# Blueprint: stuck red dot (MUST-1) + broken manual "Clear Status" (MUST-2)

## Correction to the investigation report — MUST-1's mechanism is different

The report's cause ("POST times out under host saturation; add retry/queue") is wrong, which changes the fix. Evidence from this machine:

- Host `127.0.0.1:55331`: healthy until 03:00 today, then **zero** successful posts. Hourly: `02` = 786 ok / 0 err; `03` = 1 ok / 926 err; `04`-`12` = 0 ok / 3880 err. A cliff, not 32% loss.
- `netstat -ano`: **55331 is not listening.** Live host is `48056` (pid 21352).
- `~/.superset/host/30e16b5a-.../manifest.json` → `"endpoint":"http://127.0.0.1:48056"`, rewritten 03:01. One org, one manifest.
- 6 terminals still POST to the dead 55331. All 6 have `terminal_sessions` rows in the **live** host's `host.db`; 2 are `status=active`.

This is the known **frozen hook URL** bug — `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\plans\agent-hook-url-frozen-port.md`. The env var is baked into the PTY at creation; host-service restarted onto a new port; PTYs survive in the pty-daemon with the dead address.

That plan's fix shipped **only to the shell templates**. `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\agent-setup\templates\notify-hook.template.sh:170-205` builds candidates from every `~/.superset/host/*/manifest.json`. The fork's Python hook, which owns every dot decision, was never ported: `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\apps\desktop\src\main\lib\agent-jsonl-watcher\pane-map-hook.ts:2430` reads the env var, `:2247-2253` posts to it, done.

**Why the symptom is specifically a stuck red.** `~/.claude/settings.json` routes `PermissionRequest` → `notify.sh` (has failover) and every other event → `superset-notify.py` (does not). The red-**setter** still reaches the live host; the red-**clearer** (`pane-map-hook.ts:2152-2159`) is lost. Confirmed in `~/.superset/agent-dot-render.log`: `d3ce7c25` red 10:36:33, answer 10:55:38 logged `post-error`, cleared 11:13:02 (37 min). `72d85ab1` red 10:39:05, still red past 11:14. Control terminal `d603171b` on the live host flips `permission`↔`working` in seconds.

The report's "SubagentActive flood" is a red herring — the flooded host runs 19,459 posted / 8 errors.

## Clear-status trace: confirmed, with two refinements

Your trace is correct on every line reference I checked. Two refinements:

1. **`outstandingReadyAt` and the companion ready-alert are already handled today**, not missing. The hook-local `clearWorkspaceAttention` (`...\useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceItemActions.ts:62-79`) calls `markTerminalSeenAndReportRead` per binding (`...\V2NotificationController\lib\companionAlertSync.ts:390-412`), which bumps `terminalSeenAt`, reports read to the host, and clears `outstandingReadyAt` **on acceptance**. Green and its phone alert do come down. Only red/yellow/blue survive. Widening the store purge is still worth doing as belt-and-braces for the rejected-report case, but it is not the defect.
2. **`shellRunningTerminals` genuinely needs clearing, for plain shells only.** The agent-terminal gate at `store.ts:971-974` suppresses shell-blue on agent terminals, so a stuck agent dot is unaffected. But `selectV2WorkspaceDisplayStatus:1183-1190` yields a status for open terminals with **no** source entry, so a plain shell's blue renders even after every source is purged.

Also confirmed: `clearV2TerminalRunStatus` (`store.ts:812-826`) has exactly two references — its own definition and the barrel re-export at `...\stores\v2-notifications\index.ts:2`. Dead, and its doc comment falsely claims interrupt and pane close route through it.

**Scope correction:** there is **one** Clear Status item, on sidebar workspace rows (`...\DashboardSidebarWorkspaceContextMenu.tsx:293-298`). No tab-level clear exists. The v1 `...\screens\main\...\WorkspaceListItem.tsx:223` drives the tabs store and is dead under the v2-only rule. The item *is* reachable when a red is stuck — its `hasStatus` gate reads `useV2WorkspaceDisplayStatus` (`store.ts:1196-1205`), the same store holding the latched red. The bug is the handler, not the gate.

Two re-assertion vectors survive the click, both from the surviving marker:
- `...\resyncAgentStatus.ts:678-685` re-applies `PermissionRequest` from `pendingPermission`, derived from the marker dir at `...\agent-status-snapshot.ts:127-167`. Forcing the binding to `Stop` cannot help — `pendingPermission` is deliberately independent of `lastEventType` (`agent-status-snapshot.ts:13-19`). The `pendingPermission === null` branch at `:660-677` re-applies only `preEntry.axes.permission`, so once the renderer clears locally it re-latches nothing; it is a hazard only while the local clear is missing.
- The hook's central guard: while the marker exists, untagged tool completions return `SubagentActive` (`pane-map-hook.ts:2214-2216`), which clears nothing (`statusTransitions.ts:64-74`). The red is self-perpetuating.

---

## 1. Recommended approach

### MUST-1 — port the manifest failover into the embedded Python hook

Env URL first (unchanged fast path), then each `~/.superset/host/*/manifest.json` endpoint, stopping at the first response not containing `"ignored": true`.

Alternatives and why each loses:
- **Queue + retry in the hook** (report's proposal): retrying a permanently dead port never delivers, and a background drain process is the `(HOOK-FORK-DIET)` fork cascade this fork banned.
- **Raise the 1.5 s timeout**: nothing is slow, the port is gone. Pure agent stall, zero delivery.
- **Reduce the SubagentActive flood**: the flooded host has a 0.04% error rate.
- **Marker-driven resync alone**: heals the dot but every other event from those terminals still vanishes.
- **Stable socket / port pinning**: rejected at `plans\agent-hook-url-frozen-port.md:90-91`; reasons hold.

Decisive advantage: `writeNotifyScriptIfChanged()` (`pane-map-hook.ts:2736`) rewrites the script every desktop start and Python re-reads it per fire, so **this heals the 6 already-orphaned live terminals on their next event** with no terminal restart.

`(HOOK-FORK-DIET)` risk: **none.** That footgun is `.sh` templates forking subprocesses under emulated msys2. This is one Python process doing stdlib file reads and in-process `urlopen`.

Cost: fast path unchanged. A dead env URL already costs 1.5 s (3 s when the companion retry fires, `:2623-2642`); drop the per-candidate timeout to **0.75 s** and the worst case halves while actually delivering.

### MUST-2 — make the marker the thing manual clear deletes, and give the renderer the axis clear it never performed

**Host half (authoritative).** New mutation `terminalAgents.dismissWorkspaceStatuses`: per terminal in scope, delete the `<terminalId>.askq` directory, force the binding to `Stop` via the existing `store.clearWorkspaceStatuses`, and settle any pending companion question via `markStale`.

**Renderer half.** `handleClearStatus` awaits that mutation, then calls the already-existing, uncalled `useV2NotificationStore.clearWorkspaceStatuses(workspaceId)` (`store.ts:648-690`) — your name-collision finding.

On sharing one path with the automatic answer-clear: **they cannot be one function**, because they differ on the only axis that matters — manual may drop a live red, automatic may not. What they share, and what removes the duplication that matters, is the **marker mutation and its path derivation**. The `.askq` path is built in three places today: the Python hook (`pane-map-hook.ts:602-629`), the Electron watcher (`agent-jsonl-watcher.ts:505-546`), and the host reader (`agent-status-snapshot.ts:127-138`). A fourth ad-hoc copy is exactly the parallel mechanism to avoid, so the new writer goes **in `agent-status-snapshot.ts`, sharing one path builder and one `SAFE_MARKER_SEGMENT` guard with the reader.** Full unification across Python/main/host needs a rewrite and is out of scope; I am not pretending otherwise.

Host-side rather than Electron main because the marker lives on the machine the agent runs on — for a relay or remote host, main's `homedir()` is the wrong disk. The host also already holds the binding and the companion store, so one round trip does all three.

`markStale` not `resolve`: `resolve` stamps answered-by provenance (`question-store.ts:757-761`) and nobody answered. `markStale` (`:1976-1991`) is terminal, non-provenance-bearing, and still routes through `settle()` → `onSettled` → `push.cancelPending` (`companion\index.ts:663-676`), so the phone notification retracts. Same retraction, honest record.

**Deliberately not deleted: the run-dir subagent markers.** They do not re-assert red on resync — resync replays `lastEventType`, forced to `Stop`. They only shape the *next* hook decision, and a running subagent re-asserting yellow at its next event is correct. Deleting them would corrupt live subagent tracking.

**Cross-window propagation by convergence, not broadcast.** Marker gone plus binding `Stop` means any other window's next resync converges to cleared. I considered broadcasting `Detached` (clears both axes at `statusTransitions.ts:51-62`, chime-exempt at `lifecycleEvents.ts:74-88`) — rejected, because `Detached` also means "session ended" (`map-event-type.ts:11`) and asserting it about a live agent is a lie told to fix a refresh delay.

## 2. Files to create/modify

### MUST-1

**`...\apps\desktop\src\main\lib\agent-jsonl-watcher\pane-map-hook.ts`** — embedded `NOTIFY_SCRIPT` (`:312`+):
- New `_hook_candidate_urls(env_url)` near `_post_hook` (`:2238`): `[env_url]` + `<endpoint>/trpc/notifications.hook` per manifest glob, deduped, order-preserving. Root = `os.environ.get("SUPERSET_HOME_DIR")` or `Path.home()/".superset"`. Per-manifest failure skips and logs, never raises.
- New `_deliver(candidates, body)`: POST each in order; return on the first body **not** containing `"ignored": true` / `"ignored":true`. Raise the last exception if all fail. Keying on *absence of `ignored:true`* rather than presence of `ignored:false` means an upstream body change degrades to "delivered to the first host that answered" instead of "probes forever". `notifications.ts:357` returns `ignored:false` on delivery; `:221-225`, `:275-279`, `:293-297` return `ignored:true` on drops.
- `_post_hook:2253`: timeout `1.5` → `0.75`.
- Call site `:2606-2649`: `_post_hook` → `_deliver`; log the delivering url in the `posted` record. The companion strip-and-retry at `:2623-2642` re-runs through `_deliver` with the same list.
- Total-failure path `:2643-2648`: also emit one `_decision_log` line naming every candidate tried. Reuses the always-on rotating audit channel (`:415`).
- **`_log` rotation** (`:401-412`): copy the rotate-at-1 MB idiom from `_decision_log:428-438`. Live file is **508,757,788 bytes**. MUST, not SHOULD — two lines, same function family, and an unbounded append inside the agent's hook window is itself latency.

**`...\agent-jsonl-watcher\pane-map-hook.test.ts`** — new `describe("superset-notify endpoint resolution")`. Harness at `:12-97` already gives real Python, throwaway `HOME`, real `Bun.serve` sink. Cases: dead env URL → delivered via manifest; live env URL → manifests unprobed; wrong-org sink returning `ignored:true` → probing continues; all dead → `post-error` + a `dot-decisions.log` line. The existing sink at `:43` returns no `ignored` field, which counts as delivered under the predicate, so existing tests pass unchanged — the wrong-org case must return `ignored:true` explicitly.

### MUST-2

**`...\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\notifications\agent-status-snapshot.ts`** — extract the path builder inline at `:133-138` into `askqMarkerDir(terminalId)`, used by both the existing `hasPendingQuestionMarker` and a new `clearPendingQuestionMarkers(terminalId): Promise<boolean>` (`rm` recursive+force; `ENOENT` is success; any other error throws so the caller fails loud). Reader and writer share one derivation and one `SAFE_MARKER_SEGMENT` check (`:80`).

**`...\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\terminal-agents\terminal-agents.ts`** — new `dismissWorkspaceStatuses` mutation beside `clearWorkspaceStatuses` (`:277-287`), input `{workspaceId, terminalId?}`. Per terminal, in order: `clearPendingQuestionMarkers` → existing `ctx.terminalAgentStore.clearWorkspaceStatuses` → companion dismissal. Returns `{cleared, markersRemoved, questionsDismissed}` so the renderer can surface partial failure. A marker delete that throws fails the whole mutation loud — a green UI over a surviving marker is the exact bug being fixed. `clearWorkspaceStatuses` stays **unchanged**: `useTerminalInterruptClear.ts:46` depends on its non-red-dropping semantics.

**`...\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\notifications\companion-question-sink.ts`** — add `dismissByTerminal({hostTerminalId, reason})` to the `CompanionQuestionSink` interface (`:78-87`) and a `forwardCompanionDismissal(...)` mirroring `forwardCompanionCapture` (`:264-311`). No-op when no bridge is registered.

**`...\packages\host-service\src\companion\index.ts`** — implement `dismissByTerminal` on the sink registered at `:1161`: `store.byHostTerminal(id)` then `store.markStale(id, reason)`. Both already on `QuestionStore` (`question-store.ts:749`, `:777`); no question-store change.

**`...\useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceItemActions.ts`** — `handleClearStatus` (`:292-307`) calls `dismissWorkspaceStatuses`, and **on success** the store's `clearWorkspaceStatuses(workspaceId)` plus the existing `clearWorkspaceAttention()` and query invalidation. Host first, renderer second: a failed host call must not leave a cleared-looking UI the next resync un-clears. Existing `toast.error` at `:302-306` is the loud path.

**`...\apps\desktop\src\renderer\stores\v2-notifications\store.ts`** — `clearWorkspaceStatuses` (`:648-690`) additionally clears `shellRunningTerminals` (for plain-shell blue, per the refinement above) and `outstandingReadyAt` for the workspace's terminals. Add the `ndots` mutation log line the sibling `clearWorkspaceAttention` writes at `:704-713`.

**Dead code (same PR).** Delete `clearV2TerminalRunStatus` (`store.ts:812-826`) and its barrel re-export (`...\stores\v2-notifications\index.ts:2`). Zero callers; its doc comment at `:808-811` and `:827-829` falsely documents the interrupt and pane-close paths, which is actively misleading anyone debugging exactly this bug. Deleting beats fixing the comment: the correct behaviour it describes now lives in the dismiss path and in `useTerminalInterruptClear`.

**Tests**: `...\stores\v2-notifications\store.test.ts` (all maps cleared); a router test for the host mutation (marker dir gone, binding `Stop`, `markStale` called, throw on undeletable marker).

**`FEATURES.md`** — two feature rows plus markers, tab-separated per `:150-215`:
```
(HOOK-ENDPOINT-HEAL)	apps/desktop/src/main
(HOOK-ENDPOINT-HEAL)	apps/desktop/src/main/lib/agent-jsonl-watcher/pane-map-hook.test.ts
(MANUAL-DISMISS)	packages/host-service/src
(MANUAL-DISMISS)	apps/desktop/src/renderer
```
The test-pinned marker matters: without it a merge could take upstream's router and leave the dispatch untested — the reasoning already written at `notifications.ts:16-22` for `(COMPANION-CAPTURE-HOOK)`.

### SHOULD (separable, user's call)

**A. `resyncAgentStatus.ts:660-685`** — add the missing `else` for `pendingPermission === false`, clearing the permission axis via `applySourceAxes(..., {set:[], clear:["permission"]}, row.lastEventAt)`, shaped like `:664-676`. Today resync can only add red. Safety rests on the marker being the invariant; residual risk is a question opening between the host's `readdir` and the apply. **Verify the `preEntry.occurredAt > row.lastEventAt` fence at `:460-463` covers a mid-flight `PermissionRequest` before shipping.** If not, unsafe — do not ship.

**B. `HostNotificationSubscriber.tsx:135-172`** — A only helps on reconnect: `runResync` fires on socket open or workspace-set growth. On a stable socket it never runs, which is today's stuck case. Add a bounded 60 s re-run reusing `runResync` and its generation guard.

**C. `useTerminalInterruptClear.ts:40-58`** — same host-only defect you found: clears the binding, never the renderer's latched axes. Fix by clearing the terminal's own axes locally after the mutation resolves. **Do not point it at the new dismiss mutation** — an interrupt is not a dismissal and must keep no-drop-live-red semantics. Partly mitigated today: the watcher clears `_main` on a detected interrupt (`agent-jsonl-watcher.ts:1043-1044`).

### Explicitly do not do

**Leaked `.askq/_main` reap.** 24 leaked markers, oldest 2026-07-07. The no-reap policy is argued at `agent-status-snapshot.ts:104-122`: nothing host-side can prove a leak, and the mtime-vs-`startedAt` fence drops live reds in exactly the case it targets. Not an aggravator here — markers on both stuck terminals were correct throughout. MUST-2 gives the user a deliberate way to clear them, which is the right answer.

## 3. Data flow

**MUST-1, today** — user answers → `PostToolUse` → `_remove(askq/_main)` **[local, succeeds]** → `"Start"` **[correct]** → POST `55331` **[dead, 1.5 s]** → `post-error` into a 508 MB file. Binding stays `PermissionRequest`; renderer stays red.

**MUST-1, after** — … → `"Start"` → candidates `[55331/…, 48056/…]` → `_deliver` candidate 2 → `200 {"ignored":false}` → `notifications.ts:312` broadcast, `:320` binding persisted → `statusTransitions.ts:76-87` sets working, clears permission. Sub-second.

**MUST-2, after** — right-click → Clear Status → `dismissWorkspaceStatuses` → per terminal: `rm -rf <terminalId>.askq` → binding `Stop` → `byHostTerminal` + `markStale` → `settle()` → `onSettled` → `push.cancelPending` (phone retracts). On resolve: `clearWorkspaceStatuses(workspaceId)` drops sources, background, shell, manual, outstanding-ready; `clearWorkspaceAttention()` marks seen and reports read; bindings query invalidated. Next resync reads `pendingPermission: false` and re-asserts nothing; the hook's central guard sees an empty dir and stops holding `SubagentActive`.

Validation stays at the tRPC input boundary; ownership stays the DB read (`notifications.ts:256-261`); the `.askq` directory remains the single source of truth for red.

## 4. Build order

1. `_log` rotation. Verify: suite green; shipped script rotates at 1 MB.
2. `_hook_candidate_urls`, unused. Verify: candidate list over a temp `SUPERSET_HOME_DIR` with 0/1/2 manifests plus one malformed.
3. `_deliver` + 0.75 s, wired into the call site and companion retry. Verify: four new cases; full `bun test apps/desktop/src/main/lib/agent-jsonl-watcher/`.
4. Mutation check (matching `plans\agent-hook-url-frozen-port.md:116`): break the manifest glob, confirm exactly the failover tests fail, restore.
5. **Live heal, no rebuild needed**: start the desktop so `writeNotifyScriptIfChanged` lands the script, answer a question in `72d85ab1` or `d3ce7c25`, watch `~/.superset/agent-dot-render.log` for `permission → working` within a second and a `posted` line naming `48056`.
6. `clearPendingQuestionMarkers` + shared path builder. Verify: unit test incl. traversal-guard rejection.
7. `dismissByTerminal` on sink and bridge. Verify: `markStale` fires, push retraction observed.
8. `dismissWorkspaceStatuses`. Verify: marker gone, binding `Stop`, question stale, throw on undeletable marker.
9. Renderer store widening + `handleClearStatus` + dead-code deletion. Verify: store test, then CDP per `.agents/skills/cdp-verification/SKILL.md` — stick a red, click Clear Status, confirm the dot dies and **survives a forced resync**.
10. `FEATURES.md`; `node scripts/check-feature-markers.mjs`.
11. SHOULD A (gated on the step-7 fence check in §2), then B, then C.
12. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run lint`, `/simplify` before commit.

---

## 5. Risks and edge cases

**MUST-1**
- Multi-org double delivery impossible — a terminal id lives in one host's `terminal_sessions`; others answer `ignored:true`.
- A stale manifest from a crashed host adds 0.75 s. Manifests are rewritten per start (`apps\desktop\src\main\host-service\index.ts:126-138`), one per org. Do not add a cache for a two-entry loop.
- Predicate drift: if upstream ever returns a body containing literal `"ignored": true` for a delivered event, detection inverts. Covered by tests.
- Ordering: `_deliver` must stay strictly after the marker mutations in `_decide_event_type` (`:2500-2513`) so markers survive total delivery failure.
- `SUPERSET_HOME_DIR` honoured for the manifest glob, **not** for marker paths — the host reads markers at a hardcoded `homedir()` (`agent-status-snapshot.ts:133-138`), so unifying would silently break the resync. Comment it; do not "clean it up".

**MUST-2**
- **Dropping a live red is intended** and is the user's explicit decision. A genuinely blocked agent shows red again at its next hook event; between click and event the dot lies. This is why no automatic path may reuse it.
- Marker delete racing a *new* owner mid-dismiss destroys that question's red. Bounded to milliseconds, self-heals at the next event. Do not add locking.
- Partial failure across N terminals: fail loud on the first marker error and report which succeeded. Silent partial success is what produced this bug class.
- Remote/relay hosts: markers deleted on the **host's** disk — the reason this is not in Electron main.
- Other windows lag until their next resync: ≤60 s with SHOULD-B, until reconnect without it.
- No schema change, no migration, no new persisted key (`partialize` at `store.ts:745-753` already covers the maps).
- Three similarly-named functions (host `clearWorkspaceStatuses`, renderer `clearWorkspaceStatuses`, new `dismissWorkspaceStatuses`) is a real readability hazard — the name collision already caused this bug. Cross-reference all three in comments.

---

## 6. Out of scope

- Events lost between 03:00 and the fix: gone. MUST-1 heals forward only.
- Preventing the port from moving (pinning, stable socket) — rejected in the plan doc.
- The `SubagentActive` volume — not a fault at 0.04% error.
- The other three shell templates — already fixed.
- Adding a **tab-level** Clear Status. None exists; the mutation takes an optional `terminalId` so it is cheap later, but the UI is a scope change.
- The v1 `screens/main` context menu and tabs store.
- Full unification of the four `.askq` path implementations across Python, Electron main, and host.
- Surfacing "agent status unavailable" in the UI (`plans\agent-hook-url-frozen-port.md:138`).
- Leaked-marker reaping and the `SUPERSET_AGENT_HOOK_PORT` cosmetic gap.
</agent-message>

<agent-message from="arch-review-sol">
Review verdict: the frozen-port diagnosis is correct, but MUST-2 is not safe or authoritative as written.

BLOCKER 1: recursive marker deletion needs a path boundary the blueprint omits. The current reader rejects unsafe terminal ids before building the path at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\notifications\agent-status-snapshot.ts:127-138. The existing mutation accepts any string at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\terminal-agents\terminal-agents.ts:277-285. Extracting only the path builder and calling rm({recursive:true}) on terminalId? permits ../ traversal into directories ending .askq. Put SAFE_MARKER_SEGMENT validation inside askqMarkerDir/clearPendingQuestionMarkers, validate at the zod boundary, and derive the workspace-wide terminal set from store bindings rather than raw input. Add traversal/refusal tests.

BLOCKER 2: manual clear is not durable across resync. Host clear changes lastEventType to Stop but preserves lastEventAt at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\terminal-agents\store.ts:179-199. Resync replays that Stop at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\components\V2NotificationController\lib\resyncAgentStatus.ts:473-480, which recreates review/green, and removes it only when terminalSeenAt already covers the event at :515-575. The proposed renderer clear never records a seen watermark. Therefore reconnect, another window, and SHOULD-B’s timer reintroduce a dot. The dismiss result should return affected terminal ids plus host lastEventAt, and the renderer should mark each seen through that generation, or the host must expose/broadcast a durable dismissal generation. A broad local delete alone cannot be called authoritative.

MAJOR: do not delete outstandingReadyAt in clearWorkspaceStatuses. This field is deliberately the only evidence needed to retract a ready alert after the green dot has already gone, per C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\apps\desktop\src\renderer\stores\v2-notifications\store.ts:149-177. It is cleared only after the host accepts the seen report at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\components\V2NotificationController\lib\companionAlertSync.ts:381-410. Unconditional removal strands phone/watch ready notifications. Manual clear should use markTerminalSeenAndReportRead for affected terminals, then compare-and-clear on acknowledgement.

MAJOR: CompanionQuestionSink.dismissByTerminal -> questionStore.markStale retracts push through settle/cancelPending, but it does not publish question.stale or advance gseq. Existing hook resolution publishes a frame in createNotifyingCaptureSink at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\index.ts:1779-1806. Reconcile explicitly publishes settled questions because tree freshness depends on it at :970-997 and :1019-1041. The new dismiss path needs the same event publication with a manual-dismiss reason, or the paired UI can keep stale question state.

MAJOR: _deliver’s success predicate should parse the tRPC envelope and require result.data.json.ignored === false, not accept every response lacking the text 'ignored: true'. The host’s only accepted result is explicit ignored:false at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\notifications\notifications.ts:312-357; ignored responses cover unknown terminal and wrong org at :263-297. Absence-based acceptance silently treats malformed/HTML/old responses as delivery, unlike notify.sh’s explicit ignored:false check at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\agent-setup\templates\notify-hook.template.sh:203-209. Also, 'raise last exception' is undefined when every candidate returns ignored:true and none throws. Define a delivery error carrying the last response and add an all-ignored test.

MAJOR: SHOULD-B cannot call runResync periodically without changing its epoch/key guard. runResync returns immediately when syncedKeyRef equals the key at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\components\V2NotificationController\components\HostNotificationSubscriber\HostNotificationSubscriber.tsx:139-146. Current triggers are socket-open and workspace-key change at :265-289 and :428-430. A timer must have an explicit force path, reset the guard safely, prevent overlaps, and clean up. Also, if cross-window convergence is the chosen propagation mechanism, B is MUST, not optional. There is no terminal-store change listener wired to the event bus; clearWorkspaceStatuses only mutates/emit internally.

MAJOR: SHOULD-C must preserve permission red. The existing dead helper clears the entire source, including permission, at C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\apps\desktop\src\renderer\stores\v2-notifications\store.ts:808-826. MAJOR-1 (MUST-1 predicate). "First response NOT containing ignored:true" is the wrong polarity. notify.sh keys SUCCESS on positive `"ignored":false` (template :204-206), with any-2xx as fallback (:207-214). A reused port answering 200 with a foreign body contains no ignored:true → negative predicate declares delivered → event silently lost — exactly the failure class this feature exists to fix. Python isn't constrained to substring matching like sh: json.loads the body and require ignored===false. Also handles both spacings for free.

MAJOR-2 (MUST-1 all-ignored path undefined). A ghost terminal (real precedent: agent-status-snapshot.ts:41-48 — EVERY hook POST returned ignored:true) gets ignored:true from every candidate on every event. "Raise last exception if all fail" has no exception then. As written it falls through undefined; worse, if treated as failure it triggers the companion strip-and-retry loop + post-error log on a healthy path, every event. Define: all candidates probed, ≥1 2xx, none accepted → delivered no-op (notify.sh parity), log once.

MAJOR-3 (MUST-1 timeout halving). Drop the 1.5s→0.75s change. A dead loopback port fails with instant ECONNREFUSED — failover latency does not need a shorter timeout. Halving only bites a slow-but-live host (this is an emulated-msys2 ARM64 box under load): spurious failover, duplicate lifecycle/companion delivery, strip-and-retry churn. Keep 1.5s per candidate.

MAJOR-4 (MUST-2 marker enumeration unspecified). The mutation must say WHERE the terminal list for marker deletion comes from. terminalAgentStore bindings are persistence-backed (store.ts:40-57) so they survive restart, but a binding-less terminal with a leaked marker re-asserts red the moment its binding revives — after the user explicitly dismissed. Enumerate terminal_sessions rows for the workspace (DB as source of truth) and rm each <terminalId>.askq. Carry the SAFE_MARKER_SEGMENT guard (agent-status-snapshot.ts:80,132) into the extracted askqMarkerDir — it's the traversal guard.

MAJOR-5 (MUST-2 green resurrection after dismiss). Resync re-applies row.lastEventType (Stop) per row; review-raise is only suppressed when terminalSeenAt >= lastEventAt (resyncAgentStatus.ts:515-575). Dismiss forces Stop keeping lastEventAt, and renderer clear wipes the source entry, so any terminal whose pane wasn't visible comes back GREEN on next resync — within 60s if SHOULD-B ships. Clear Status would trade stuck-red for returning-green. Fix inside the design: on dismiss success also markTerminalSeen(terminalId, row.lastEventAt) for the affected terminals (renderer has the primitive; resync itself calls it at :509-511).

SHOULD-A GATE: verified sound. Single loop 431-686 (brace-scanned), fence `continue` at :460-463 skips the pendingPermission block at :660-685; loop is synchronous so no mid-loop event interleave. Ship A — recommend mirroring the null-branch's workspaceId check (:664-666) in the else-branch. Note: WITHOUT A, the blueprint's cross-window "convergence" claim is weaker than stated — nothing clears the permission axis on pendingPermission=false except A itself; other windows rely on the Stop apply passing the strict-> fence (equal timestamps pass, so it likely converges, but that's the one convergence claim I could not fully prove). One more reason to ship A.

<agent-message from="arch-design">
# FINAL IMPLEMENTATION SPEC — stuck red dot + manual "Clear Status"

Scope approved: MUST-1, MUST-2, SHOULD-A, SHOULD-B, SHOULD-C. All consensus amendments adopted. One reviewer premise is factually wrong and I have the measurement; the resulting decision still goes the reviewers' way.

---

## 0. Disagreement resolved: keep 1.5 s. Fable's conclusion is right, its premise is wrong.

Fable argued a dead loopback port gives instant ECONNREFUSED, so failover needs no shorter timeout. **Measured on this box just now:**

```
55331 (dead, was the host)  2043.6 ms  ConnectionRefusedError [WinError 10061]
59999 (dead, never used)    2039.5 ms  ConnectionRefusedError [WinError 10061]
48056 (live host)               0.6 ms  connected
```

A dead loopback port takes **~2040 ms** to refuse here, not microseconds. That is precisely why 5,099 of 5,103 logged failures read `<urlopen error timed out>` and only 3 read anything else — the 1.5 s deadline expires ~540 ms *before* Windows surfaces the RST. The hook has never once seen a refusal.

**Decision: keep `timeout=1.5`.** My 0.75 s proposal is withdrawn, for reasons the measurement supplies rather than Fable's:

- 1.5 s already caps the dead-port cost *below* the 2040 ms OS refusal, so shortening buys 750 ms, not 2 s. Small prize.
- Against that: 0.6 ms live vs 750 ms budget looks like a huge margin, but I have **no p99 measurement of live-host response latency** on this emulated ARM64 box, and the healthy host's 8 errors in 19,459 posts prove slow responses exist. Trading an unmeasured correctness risk for 750 ms is a bad trade.
- "Never worse than today" is the right bar for a fix. 1.5 s meets it exactly.

Two bounds adopted from Sol instead:

- **Total probe budget 4000 ms** across all candidates, checked before each attempt. Worst realistic case (2 candidates) = 3 s, inside budget; a pathological manifest set cannot turn one hook event into a 10 s stall. On budget exhaustion: stop, log, treat as undelivered.
- **Org-first candidate ordering.** `SUPERSET_ORGANIZATION_ID` is set on the terminal env at `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\terminal\env.ts:256-257`. Order = env URL, then that org's manifest, then remaining manifests. Keeps the common list at 2 and avoids foreign-org round trips.

---

## 1. Files to modify

### MUST-1 — endpoint healing in the Python hook

**`C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\apps\desktop\src\main\lib\agent-jsonl-watcher\pane-map-hook.ts`**, embedded `NOTIFY_SCRIPT` (`:312`+). Marker `(HOOK-ENDPOINT-HEAL)` on each new function.

`_hook_candidate_urls(env_url)` — new, near `_post_hook` (`:2238`):
- Root = `os.environ.get("SUPERSET_HOME_DIR")` or `Path.home()/".superset"`. **Manifest glob only** — marker paths keep `Path.home()`, because the host reads them at a hardcoded `homedir()` (`...\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\notifications\agent-status-snapshot.ts:133-138`). Comment this; do not unify.
- Order: `env_url`, then `<home>/host/<SUPERSET_ORGANIZATION_ID>/manifest.json`, then `<home>/host/*/manifest.json`. Dedupe preserving first position.
- Endpoint → `<endpoint>/trpc/notifications.hook`. Per-manifest failure (missing, unreadable, bad JSON, no `endpoint`) skips that entry and logs; never raises.

`_deliver(candidates, body)` — new. Per candidate, while `elapsed < 4.0`:
- `_post_hook(url, body)` (timeout stays 1.5).
- **Positive acceptance only.** `json.loads(response_body)`, then require `parsed["result"]["data"]["json"]["ignored"] is False`. Anything else — a `True`, a missing key, a non-dict, unparseable text, HTML from a foreign server on a recycled port — is **not** delivery. This matches notify.sh's positive check at `...\packages\agent-setup\templates\notify-hook.template.sh:204-206` and the envelope the route returns (`notifications.ts:312-357`; the test sink at `pane-map-hook.test.ts:43` returns exactly this shape).
- Track `saw_2xx` separately for the all-ignored path.
- Returns one of three outcomes: `("delivered", url, status, body)`, `("ignored-everywhere", None, None, None)` when every candidate was probed, at least one answered 2xx, and none accepted, or raises the last transport exception when nothing answered.

**All-ignored is a delivered no-op** (amendment 2, notify.sh parity at `notify-hook.template.sh:212-214`). This is the ghost-terminal case the snapshot documents at `agent-status-snapshot.ts:41-48`. Emit exactly one `_log` line with `action: "ignored-everywhere"` and **return**. It must not trigger the companion strip-and-retry and must not log `post-error` — otherwise a wedged pane writes two error records per tool call forever.

Call site `:2606-2649`:
- `_post_hook` → `_deliver`; the `posted` record gains the delivering `url`.
- Companion strip-and-retry (`:2623-2642`) fires **only** on the raise path, and re-posts to **the candidate list already computed**, not a fresh probe. If `_deliver` raised because nothing answered, a stripped body will not answer either — skip the retry and go straight to the failure log.
- Failure path `:2643-2648`: keep the `post-error` line, and add one `_decision_log` line naming every candidate tried. `_decision_log` (`:415`) is always-on and already rotates at 1 MB.

`_log` rotation (`:401-412`) — MUST. Copy the rotate-at-1 MB idiom from `_decision_log:428-438`. The live file is **508,757,788 bytes**.

Codex is covered by the same script: `hookCommand()` / `notifyHookCommand()` at `pane-map-hook.ts:2660-2668` install one Python file for both agents. No separate Codex change.

### MUST-2 — authoritative manual dismissal

**`...\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\notifications\agent-status-snapshot.ts`** — marker `(MANUAL-DISMISS)`.

Extract `askqMarkerDir(terminalId): string | null` from the inline construction at `:133-138`. **The `SAFE_MARKER_SEGMENT` test (`:80`) moves inside it** and returns `null` on failure, so no caller can build a path from an unvalidated id. `hasPendingQuestionMarker` uses it (a `null` means "definitively absent", its current `:132` behaviour).

New `clearPendingQuestionMarkers(terminalId, dismissStartedAtMs): Promise<{removed: string[], survivors: string[]}>`:
- `askqMarkerDir` → `null` ⇒ return empty/empty. Never `rm` from an id that failed the guard.
- `readdir`; `ENOENT` ⇒ empty/empty (success). Any other read error ⇒ **throw** (loud; an unknown filesystem is not a successful dismissal).
- **Per-owner mtime fence (amendment 8).** `stat` each owner; unlink only those with `mtimeMs <= dismissStartedAtMs`. A `stat` failure means the owner is **not** removed — unknown is not evidence, matching the safe direction the reader already takes at `:174-191`.
- Unlink the owner files, not the directory, then `rmdir` only if empty.

The fence is the honest answer to amendment 8, and it is better than documenting the race. `pane-map-hook.ts:2137-2141` writes the marker synchronously *before* returning `PermissionRequest`, so a question raised after the click has an mtime strictly greater than `dismissStartedAtMs` and **survives**. The semantics land exactly where they should: a dismissal clears what the user was looking at when they clicked, and nothing raised afterwards. Same wall clock, same process, NTFS mtime granularity ~100 ns.

**`...\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\terminal-agents\terminal-agents.ts`** — new `dismissWorkspaceStatuses`, marker `(MANUAL-DISMISS)`.

- Input `z.object({ workspaceId: z.string().min(1), terminalId: z.string().min(1).optional() })`. Boundary validation per amendment 3; `SAFE_MARKER_SEGMENT` is enforced again inside the path builder.
- **Terminal set derived server-side from the DB** (amendment 3): `terminalSessions` rows where `originWorkspaceId = workspaceId`, intersected with `terminalId` when supplied. Same DB-as-truth read the snapshot already performs at `agent-status-snapshot.ts:249-259`. This sweeps leaked markers of terminals with no live binding, which a bindings-derived set would miss.
- `dismissStartedAtMs = Date.now()` captured once, before any deletion, and used as the fence for every terminal.
- Per terminal, in order: `clearPendingQuestionMarkers` → existing `ctx.terminalAgentStore.clearWorkspaceStatuses(workspaceId, terminalId)` → companion dismissal.
- Returns `{ dismissStartedAtMs, terminals: Array<{ terminalId, lastEventAt, markersRemoved: number, pendingAfter: boolean, questionDismissed: boolean }> }`. `lastEventAt` is the host-clock instant from the binding after the clear (amendment 4). `pendingAfter` is `survivors.length > 0`.
- First marker error aborts the whole mutation loud, reporting which terminals completed. Silent partial success is the bug class being fixed.

`clearWorkspaceStatuses` stays **unchanged** — `useTerminalInterruptClear.ts:46` depends on its non-red-dropping semantics.

**`...\packages\host-service\src\trpc\router\notifications\companion-question-sink.ts`** — add to `CompanionQuestionSink` (`:78-87`):

```ts
dismissByTerminal(input: { hostTerminalId: string; reason: string }): boolean;
```

Plus `forwardCompanionDismissal(...)` mirroring `forwardCompanionCapture` (`:264-311`). **Sink null ⇒ return `false`, never throw** (amendment 5) — that is every build where the bridge is not running.

**`...\packages\host-service\src\companion\index.ts`** — implement it on the `createNotifyingCaptureSink` wrapper registered at `:1161-1168`, beside the existing `capture` and `resolve` (`:1770-1806`):
- `deps.questions.byHostTerminal(hostTerminalId)`; `null` ⇒ return `false`.
- `deps.inner`/`questions.markStale(question.questionId, reason)` — terminal and provenance-free (`question-store.ts:1976-1991`). Not `resolve`: that stamps answered-by provenance (`:757-761`) and nobody answered. `markStale` routes through `settle()` → `onSettled` → `push.cancelPending` (`:663-676`), so the phone notification retracts.
- **Publish the frame** (amendment 5): `events.publish({ t: "question.stale", d: { questionId, reason: QUESTION_STALE_MANUAL_DISMISS_REASON } })`, matching the reconcile path at `:985-990`. `QuestionStaleEventData` is `{questionId, reason: string}` (`types.ts:1352-1355`), so a new reason constant is a drop-in; add it beside `QUESTION_STALE_TERMINAL_GONE_REASON` (`question-store.ts:1483`). `events.publish` advances `gseq`, so paired phones refetch instead of holding stale question state.

**`...\apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\_dashboard\components\DashboardSidebar\components\DashboardSidebarWorkspaceItem\hooks\useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceItemActions\useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceItemActions.ts`** — rewrite `handleClearStatus` (`:292-307`). Ordering decided explicitly (Fable MINOR):

1. `clearWorkspaceAttention()` **first, unchanged**. Today this runs before the try block, so a host-down click still clears green and reports read. Moving it behind the mutation would regress that. Keep today's behaviour intact.
2. `await dismissWorkspaceStatuses`.
3. On success: `useV2NotificationStore.getState().clearWorkspaceStatuses(workspaceId)`, then per returned terminal `markTerminalSeenAndReportRead({ workspaceId, terminalId, lastEventAt })`.
4. On failure: existing `toast.error` (`:302-306`). Green is already cleared; red and yellow stay. **This is the correct outcome, not a regression** — the marker survived, so a locally-dropped red would be re-raised by the next resync. Showing the user a red that is still真 beats a green that lies for 60 s.

**Amendment 4 — durability.** Step 3's `markTerminalSeenAndReportRead` is what makes convergence provable. Forcing the binding to `Stop` while preserving `lastEventAt` means a later resync replays `Stop` → `review` for any terminal whose seen watermark does not cover `lastEventAt` (`...\resyncAgentStatus.ts:515-575`), and SHOULD-B would re-raise that green within 60 s. Marking seen through the host's own `lastEventAt` closes it, and it is the same primitive resync itself uses at `:509-511`.

`markTerminalSeenAndReportRead` (`...\V2NotificationController\lib\companionAlertSync.ts:390-412`) is also the answer to **Sol's MAJOR on `outstandingReadyAt`**: it reports read and compare-and-clears the record **only on host ack**, exactly as the type doc requires (`store.ts:150-177`). One call satisfies both.

**`...\apps\desktop\src\renderer\stores\v2-notifications\store.ts`** — `clearWorkspaceStatuses` (`:648-690`):
- **Add** `shellRunningTerminals` purge for the workspace's terminals. The agent-terminal gate at `:971-974` suppresses shell-blue on agent terminals, but `selectV2WorkspaceDisplayStatus:1183-1190` yields a status for open terminals with no source entry, so a plain shell's blue survives a full source purge.
- **Do NOT touch `outstandingReadyAt`** — reversing my earlier spec, per Sol. It is the retraction evidence for ready alerts and is cleared only by `clearOutstandingReady` on host ack (`:163-172`). Unconditional deletion strands a phone notification until expiry.
- Add the `ndots` mutation log line the sibling `clearWorkspaceAttention` writes at `:704-713`.

**Dead code, same PR.** Delete `clearV2TerminalRunStatus` (`store.ts:812-826`) and its barrel re-export (`...\stores\v2-notifications\index.ts:2`). Those are its only two references. Its doc comment at `:808-811` and `:827-829` claims interrupt and pane close route through it — false, and actively misleading anyone debugging this exact bug. Delete rather than correct: the behaviour it describes now lives in the dismiss path and in SHOULD-C.

### SHOULD-A — resync can retract red

**`...\V2NotificationController\lib\resyncAgentStatus.ts:660-685`.** Fable verified the gate: the `preEntry.occurredAt > row.lastEventAt` fence at `:460-463` covers a mid-flight `PermissionRequest` and the loop is synchronous. **Ship it.**

Add the `else` branch for `pendingPermission === false`, mirroring the null-branch's `workspaceId` check at `:664-666`:

```
else if (preEntry?.axes.permission !== undefined
      && preEntry.workspaceId === row.originWorkspaceId) {
  applySourceAxes(source, row.originWorkspaceId,
                  { set: [], clear: ["permission"] }, row.lastEventAt);
  result.retractedPermission++;
}
```

A also supplies the cross-window convergence proof for the permission axis that MUST-2 otherwise lacks: after a dismissal the marker is gone, so every other window's next snapshot reads `pendingPermission: false` and actively retracts. Without A, other windows converge on green/yellow but a stale red persists until reconnect.

### SHOULD-B — periodic resync

**`...\HostNotificationSubscriber\HostNotificationSubscriber.tsx:135-172`.** Ships only together with amendment 4 (which is in MUST-2), else it re-raises dismissed greens on a 60 s cadence.

`runResync` early-returns on `syncedKeyRef.current === key` (`:141-143`), so a tick must clear that guard. Requirements (amendment 6):
- `resyncInFlightRef` set before the call, cleared in both `.then` paths, so a slow snapshot cannot overlap the next tick.
- The tick sets `syncedKeyRef.current = null` immediately before invoking `runResync`, and only when connected and not in flight.
- 60_000 ms interval, created on connect and **cleared on disconnect and on unmount**, alongside the existing `retryTimerRef` cleanup.
- The existing generation guard (`resyncGenerationRef`, `:145`) already discards a superseded reply; do not add a second mechanism.

### SHOULD-C — interrupt clears the renderer too

**`...\TerminalPane\hooks\useTerminalInterruptClear\useTerminalInterruptClear.ts:40-58`.** After the existing mutation resolves, clear that terminal's own axes locally.

**Clear `working` and `review` and the background-running entry. Never `permission`** (amendment 7). An interrupt is an automatic path; dropping a live red there is the one thing forbidden. Ctrl+C does not answer a pending AskUserQuestion, and the watcher's `_main` clear on a detected interrupt (`...\agent-jsonl-watcher.ts:1043-1044`) is the only sanctioned red release on that path.

Do **not** route this at `dismissWorkspaceStatuses`. Keep `clearWorkspaceStatuses`.

### FEATURES.md

Two human-readable rows in the feature table (`:15`+), and four machine lines in the fenced ```markers block (`:110-120`, format `<marker-token>\t<path-glob-root>`; two lines with different roots is legal):

The test-pinned line matters: without it a merge could take upstream's router and leave the dispatch untested while every gate stays green — the reasoning already written at `notifications.ts:16-22` for `(COMPANION-CAPTURE-HOOK)`.

## 2. Data flow

**MUST-1, after.** Answer → `PostToolUse` → `_remove(askq/<owner>)` → `"Start"` → candidates `[55331/…, 48056/…]` → candidate 1 transport-fails at 1.5 s → candidate 2 → `200`, envelope parsed, `ignored === false` → accepted → `notifications.ts:312` broadcast, `:320` binding persisted → `statusTransitions.ts:76-87` sets working, clears permission.

**MUST-2, after.** Right-click → Clear Status → local `clearWorkspaceAttention()` (green + read report) → `dismissWorkspaceStatuses`: `dismissStartedAtMs` captured; terminal set read from `terminalSessions`; per terminal, owners with `mtime <= dismissStartedAtMs` unlinked (newer ones survive) → binding forced `Stop` → `byHostTerminal` + `markStale` → `settle()` → `push.cancelPending` + `question.stale` frame (gseq advances, phone refetches) → returns per-terminal `{lastEventAt, pendingAfter}`. Renderer: `clearWorkspaceStatuses(workspaceId)` purges sources, background, shell, manual; `markTerminalSeenAndReportRead` per terminal through `lastEventAt`. Next resync reads `pendingPermission: false`, `lastEventType: "Stop"` covered by the seen watermark, and asserts nothing. The hook's central guard sees an empty dir and stops holding `SubagentActive` (`pane-map-hook.ts:2214-2216`).

Validation at the tRPC boundary plus inside the path builder; ownership from the DB read; the `.askq` directory remains the single source of truth for red.

---

## 3. Build order

1. `_log` rotation. Verify: suite green, shipped script rotates at 1 MB.
2. `_hook_candidate_urls` (unused). Verify: 0/1/2 manifests, one malformed, org-first ordering, dedupe.
3. `_deliver` with positive envelope parsing, all-ignored outcome, 4 s budget. Wire the call site and the constrained companion retry. Verify: full test list §5.
4. Mutation check per `plans\agent-hook-url-frozen-port.md:116`: break the manifest glob, confirm only the failover tests fail, restore.
5. **Live heal, no rebuild needed.** Start the desktop so `writeNotifyScriptIfChanged` (`pane-map-hook.ts:2736`) lands the script; answer a question in `72d85ab1` or `d3ce7c25`; expect `permission → working` in `~/.superset/agent-dot-render.log` within a second and a `posted` line naming `48056`.
6. `askqMarkerDir` + `clearPendingQuestionMarkers` with the mtime fence.
7. `dismissByTerminal` on sink and wrapper, with `question.stale` publish.
8. `dismissWorkspaceStatuses`.
9. Renderer: store widening, `handleClearStatus`, dead-code deletion.
10. SHOULD-A.
11. SHOULD-B (after A, so a tick converges rather than oscillates).
12. SHOULD-C.
13. FEATURES.md; `node scripts/check-feature-markers.mjs`.
14. `bun run typecheck`, `bun run lint`, `/simplify`.

---

## 4. Risks

- **Duplicate delivery** if a slow live host processes a request we abandoned and the next candidate also accepts. Two identical broadcasts: harmless on the dot axes, and the companion path is already protected — the retry re-sends the *same* `companionLifecycleEventId`, which the sink matches against its applied-id window (`pane-map-hook.ts:2543-2555`). Bounded further by keeping 1.5 s.
- **Foreign server on a recycled port** answering 200 with arbitrary text: rejected by positive envelope parsing. This is the concrete reason the polarity flip matters.
- **All-ignored** on a genuinely wedged pane: one log line per event, no error spam, no retry. Matches notify.sh.
- **Marker fence residual**: a question whose marker write and our `stat` interleave inside one mtime tick. NTFS granularity ~100 ns against a multi-millisecond mutation; not a practical window. A `stat` failure never deletes.
- **Dismissal is still allowed to drop a live red** — the reds that existed at click time. That is the user's explicit decision. The fence narrows it to exactly that set; it is no longer an unbounded race, and I am not claiming it self-heals.
- **`pendingAfter: true`** means a newer question survived the dismissal. The renderer must not present that terminal as cleared; surface it rather than hide it.
- **Total probe budget** could in principle truncate a legitimate multi-org probe. 4 s against two 1.5 s candidates leaves headroom; log on exhaustion.
- **Host-down click**: green clears, red/yellow stay, toast fires. Deliberate, stated above.
- **Three similarly-named functions** (host `clearWorkspaceStatuses`, renderer `clearWorkspaceStatuses`, new `dismissWorkspaceStatuses`). The collision already caused this bug — cross-reference all three in comments.
- No schema change, no migration, no new persisted key (`partialize`, `store.ts:745-753`, already covers the maps).

---

## 5. Test list

**Hook (`pane-map-hook.test.ts`, real Python + real sink, harness `:12-97`)**
1. `_log` rotates past 1 MB, single `.log.1` backup.
2. Dead env URL → delivered via manifest.
3. Live env URL → manifests never probed.
4. Wrong-org sink returns `ignored:true`, right-org returns `ignored:false` → probing continues, delivers to the right one.
5. All candidates return `ignored:true` → `action: "ignored-everywhere"`, exactly one log line, no `post-error`, no companion retry.
6. Malformed body (HTML, truncated JSON, `{}`, `{"result":{}}`) → **not** delivered; probing continues.
7. All candidates transport-fail → `post-error` plus one `dot-decisions.log` line naming every candidate.
8. Probe budget exhausted → stops, logs, no further attempts.
9. Org-first ordering with two manifests present.
10. Existing suite unchanged — note the sink at `:43` returns no `ignored` field, so it now counts as **not** delivered; update it to `{success:true, ignored:false}` or the existing tests will break. Flagging this because it is easy to miss.

**Host**
11. Unsafe terminal id (`../`, empty, unicode) → `askqMarkerDir` returns null, nothing unlinked, mutation still succeeds for valid siblings.
12. `readdir` ENOENT → success; non-ENOENT → throws.
13. mtime fence: owner older than `dismissStartedAtMs` removed; owner newer survives; `pendingAfter: true` reported.
14. `stat` failure → owner retained.
15. Workspace isolation: terminals of another workspace untouched.
16. Terminal with a `terminal_sessions` row but no live binding → its leaked marker is still swept.
17. Partial failure: first terminal throws → mutation fails, response names completed terminals.
18. `markStale` fires, `push.cancelPending` called, `question.stale` frame published with the manual reason, gseq advanced.
19. Sink null → `questionDismissed: false`, no throw.

**Renderer**
20. Store `clearWorkspaceStatuses` purges sources, background, shell, manual; leaves `outstandingReadyAt` intact.
21. `handleClearStatus` on host failure → green cleared, red/yellow retained, toast shown, no store purge.
22. `handleClearStatus` on success → axes gone, `markTerminalSeenAndReportRead` called per returned terminal with the host `lastEventAt`.
23. Resync-after-dismiss: snapshot with `pendingPermission: false` and `lastEventType: "Stop"` raises neither red nor green.
24. SHOULD-A: `pendingPermission: false` retracts a latched red; wrong-workspace `preEntry` does not; the `:460-463` fence still wins for a newer local `PermissionRequest`.
25. SHOULD-B: tick resets the guard and runs; overlapping tick suppressed while in flight; interval cleared on disconnect and unmount.
26. SHOULD-C: interrupt clears working/review/background and **leaves permission set**.
27. Cross-window convergence: window B's next resync clears after window A dismisses.

**End-to-end (CDP, `.agents/skills/cdp-verification/SKILL.md`)**
28. Stick a red, click Clear Status, confirm the dot dies and **survives a forced resync**.

DEVIATIONS (all deliberate)
- Trap (j) handled, plus one more the spec did not flag: the EXISTING `hook()` helper inherits `process.env`, and this suite runs inside Superset, so the real `SUPERSET_ORGANIZATION_ID` / `SUPERSET_HOME_DIR` would have pointed the new failover at the developer's LIVE hosts. Both are now blanked in that helper.
- Logic split into `_hook_accepted` and `_undelivered` helpers rather than inline in `_deliver`. Structural only.
- The raise still carries the last transport exception, with `superset_saw_2xx` / `superset_tried_urls` set on it (synthetic `RuntimeError` when nothing threw). That attribute is how the call site skips the companion retry when nothing answered.
- `posted` keeps `"url"` = env URL and ADDS `"deliveredUrl"`, so the existing field does not silently change meaning.
- `endpoint.rstrip("/")` before appending `/trpc/notifications.hook`. Hosts write no trailing slash, so behaviour is identical; it just cannot produce `//` or a near-duplicate candidate.
- Spec did not define what happens if the companion strip-and-retry itself returns ignored-everywhere. Falling through to post-error would be wrong (the dot WAS delivered), so it logs `companion-rejected-dot-ignored-everywhere` and returns.
- Budget test uses sinks that sleep 3s rather than dead ports, so the three 1.5s timeouts are deterministic on any OS instead of depending on how fast it refuses a connection.
- Ran `bunx biome format --write` on the test file ONLY (biome rejected my multi-line `it(name, fn, timeout)` formatting). No repo-wide run.

TWO THINGS FOR INTEGRATION
- If `SUPERSET_HOST_AGENT_HOOK_URL` is empty/unset, `main()` still returns early at `skip-no-url` BEFORE candidates are built, so failover never runs for an env-less terminal. Left as-is (spec scoped the change to :2606-2649); say the word if you want the early return moved below the candidate build.
- `_post_hook` truncates the response body at 500 chars before the acceptance parse. The real envelope is ~60 chars, so no false negative today, but a much larger success body would read as undelivered (cost: a duplicate POST plus a spurious post-error line, not a lost dot).

<agent-message from="cr-fable">
Adversarial review of uncommitted diff. Verified every finding against code; ran all new/touched suites (all green: host-service dismiss 28/28, renderer 90, hook failover+subscriber 35, react-mock fixes 5/5). Findings:

[BLOCKER] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\index.ts:1830 (dismissByTerminal) + terminal-agents.ts:229
Defect: Companion dismissal is NOT fenced by dismissStartedAtMs — `dismissByTerminal` marks stale whatever question is pending at call time, including one raised AFTER the click.
Failure: User clicks Clear Status at T0. Hook lands a new AskUserQuestion at T1>T0 (window = tRPC round trip + readdir/stat/unlink loop). `clearPendingQuestionMarkers` correctly keeps the T1 marker (survivor) — but `forwardCompanionDismissal` runs unconditionally afterward, `byHostTerminal` returns the NEW pending question, `markStale` settles it permanently. Phone alert retracted, `/v1/answer` → 410, question unanswerable from companion forever, while desk red legitimately re-asserts. Directly violates the "question raised after dismiss must survive" invariant and FEATURES.md's own claim ("a question raised after the click survives").
Fix: Pass dismissStartedAtMs into dismissByTerminal; skip when `question.askedAtMs > dismissStartedAtMs` (PendingQuestion already carries askedAtMs). Also gate the call on `cleared.survivors.length === 0`.

[MAJOR] apps\desktop\src\renderer\...\useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceItemActions.ts:138 (applyWorkspaceStatusDismissal)
Defect: Renderer purge is workspace-wide and unfenced — `clearWorkspaceStatuses(workspaceId)` drops a red latched from a post-click question even when the host reports it `pendingAfter: true`; the flag only gates seen-marking, not the purge.
Failure: New PermissionRequest broadcast latches red between click and mutation reply. Reply applies → purge wipes that red. The blocked agent fires no further hook, so the red stays hidden until the 60s periodic resync re-derives it from the surviving marker. Up to a minute of green-looking dot over a blocked question. Compounding hole server-side (terminal-agents.ts:198): an owner created between `readdir` and return is never listed → `pendingAfter: false` → terminal also marked seen.
Fix: For terminals with `pendingAfter: true`, clear per-terminal axes except permission (reuse the axis-clear used by clearInterruptedTerminalAxes) instead of relying on the workspace-wide purge; or trigger an immediate resync after dismissal instead of waiting for the periodic tick.

[MAJOR] apps\desktop\src\main\lib\agent-jsonl-watcher\pane-map-hook.ts:2837 (retry gate) + _deliver
Defect: The companion strip-and-retry is dead code for its designed trigger: an HTTP 400 from the owning host raises urllib HTTPError inside `_deliver` (exception path — status never inspected), so `superset_saw_2xx` stays False and `has_companion and getattr(exc, "superset_saw_2xx", False)` skips the retry in the single-host case.
Failure: Companion capture shape vs route schema drift (the exact scenario the retry was built for, per the comment at :2830) → every Stop carries companionLifecycleEventId → every Stop 400s → strip-retry never fires → binding never updated → yellow stuck; resync replays the stale binding so it never heals. Worse with a second org host: owner 400s, foreign host answers 200 `ignored:true` → `_deliver` returns "ignored-everywhere" → main() logs a benign delivered-no-op and returns, no retry, no post-error. Breaks the documented guarantee that a payload change "can degrade the companion feature but can NEVER move a dot". No test covers 400→strip-retry (existing assertions only check the retry does NOT fire).
Fix: In `_deliver`, catch urllib.error.HTTPError separately and record a `saw_http_response` flag (and don't let an owner's 4xx be masked by a foreign host's 200-ignored); gate the strip-retry on "any candidate returned an HTTP response" rather than 2xx-with-unaccepted-body.

## Q1 — QUESTION notification, answered on the DESKTOP

### Structured AskUserQuestion answered at the desk → **WORKS, phone AND watch**

Chain, every hop verified:

1. `PostToolUse:AskUserQuestion` → `_companion_resolved` → `{toolUseId}` — `apps/desktop/src/main/lib/agent-jsonl-watcher/pane-map-hook.ts:2787-2800`, attached at `:3002-3004`.
2. Hook POST → `forwardCompanionCapture` → `sink.resolve({hostTerminalId, toolUseId, resolvedAtMs})` — `packages/host-service/src/trpc/router/notifications/companion-question-sink.ts:333-339`.
3. Bridge sink → `deps.inner.resolve(input)` — `packages/host-service/src/companion/index.ts:1804`.
4. Store `resolve()` → `settle()` → `deps.onSettled(question)` — `packages/host-service/src/companion/question-store.ts:1618`. This is `(SETTLE-CHOKE-POINT)`: the ONLY place a terminal state is written, so no settle route can forget.
5. `onSettled` → `sender.cancelPending(questionId)` — `packages/host-service/src/companion/index.ts:669-683`.
6. `cancelPending` → `sendRetraction` → `buildRetractPushData` → FCM broadcast — `packages/host-service/src/companion/push.ts:2773-2782`, `:2475-2507`. Cancels an in-flight original (`:2489-2492`) and queues behind it on the same per-question chain, so retraction can't be overtaken.
7. Phone: v1 `k="r"` → `PushRouting.kt:59-60` → `CompanionNotifications.retract()` → `manager.cancel(questionId, ID_QUESTION)` — `app-phone/.../notifications/CompanionNotifications.kt:384-386`. Matches on `i` alone.
8. Watch: same route line `:60` → `tellWatchResolved` → `WatchBridge.pushResolved` (`WatchBridge.kt:250-266`) → `/companion/v1/question/resolved` → `app-watch/.../datalayer/CompanionWearListenerService.kt:75-80` → `QuestionNotifier.cancel`.

Desk answers are the ORIGIN of this path, not an afterthought — `_companion_resolved`'s own comment says so (`pane-map-hook.ts:2788-2792`).

### Plain typed answers (prose, not the tool) → **no notification exists to retract**

`_companion_question` returns `None` unless `PreToolUse` + `AskUserQuestion` (`pane-map-hook.ts:2740-2742`). Free-text prompts and permission prompts never mint a companion question record, so they never buzz. Not a gap — out of scope by construction.

### Dead hook / interrupted / aborted question → **WORKS, but only while the phone is polling**

`reconcile()` re-reads the transcript, finds the `tool_result`, and settles `resolved` with `surface: "desktop"` — `question-store.ts:2116-2121`. Same settle seam → same retraction. Terminal killed with no answer: settles `stale` after TWO observations `QUESTION_EXPIRY_CORROBORATION_MS` = 5 min apart (`question-store.ts:1470`, `:2096-2110`), also retracts.

**Caveat:** `reconcile()` is called from exactly one place — the `/v1/heartbeat` handler, `packages/host-service/src/companion/read-api.ts:1675`. It is phone-driven, not a host timer. A phone that has stopped heartbeating never triggers the backstop. That is the case where the backstop is needed AND the case where it doesn't run.

### Manual dismiss (right-click Clear Status) → **WORKS**

`forwardCompanionDismissal` → `dismissByTerminal` → `markStale` → settle → retract — `companion-question-sink.ts:362-372`, `index.ts:1862-1890`. Correctly `markStale` not `resolve` (no provenance to fabricate). Fenced on `dismissStartedAtMs`: a question raised at-or-after the click is left alone (`index.ts:1863-1875`), so a fresh question isn't retracted out from under a blocked agent.

### Partial keystroke write from the phone → **WORKS**

`answer.ts:1267` `markStale` on unconfirmed write → settle → retract.

4. Blind fallback if no held alert (survives a host restart): `retractById` with a recomputed id, `:1264-1274`.
5. Phone: v3 `k="c"` → `LifecycleRouting.kt:278-289` → `SeenLifecycleEvents.suppress` + `CompanionNotifications.cancelNotification` (`:297-311`) + `dismissOnWatch`.
6. Watch: `WatchBridge.pushLifecycleDismiss` → `/companion/v1/lifecycle/dismiss` → `CompanionWearListenerService.kt:121-128` → `LifecycleNotifier.cancel`.

**Which desktop interactions report — exactly three:**

| trigger | file:line |
|---|---|
| Active pane changes in the open workspace (open workspace, switch tab, click a different pane) | `.../v2-workspace/$workspaceId/hooks/useClearActivePaneAttention/useClearActivePaneAttention.ts:58` |
| Sidebar "Mark read" | `.../useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceItemActions.ts:226` |
| Right-click → Clear Status | `.../useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceItemActions.ts:176` |

**Do NOT report:** window focus, typing in the terminal, scrolling, reading an already-active pane without a pane change. If the pane was already active when the green arrived, the effect does re-fire on the status flip (`useClearActivePaneAttention.ts:64` deps) — even with the app backgrounded, which is a false-positive retraction, not a linger.

### Green cleared by NEW work (agent starts again) → **WORKS as designed, alert stays up on purpose**

Renderer: `statusTransitions.ts:76-85` deletes the review entry but leaves `outstandingReadyAt` standing (`store.ts:586-599`, `:750-755`). Host: `outcome:"progress"` → `supersede({kind:"new-cycle"})` (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:1145-1147`) → `notifiesPhone` returns **false** for `g`+`new-cycle` (`:122-124`) → the card is left `standing` for the next `g` to replace in place. That is the `(ONE-BUZZ-UNTIL-READ)` contract. The later pane-open reports via the surviving `outstandingAt` and retracts then.

### GAPS on the ready path

**GAP R1 — terminal exit / pane close. Unrecoverable.**
`lifecycleEvents.ts:194-202` calls `pruneTerminalSeen` (deletes `outstandingReadyAt`, `store.ts:402-406`) and `clearSources`, with no report. The host retracts only on a hook-delivered `outcome:"session-end"` (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:1149-1150`), whose only producer is the agent's own notify hook. A pty killed/crashed/closed with no SessionEnd hook → phone keeps the ready card to TTL, and the local evidence needed for the resync repair is destroyed.

**GAP R2 — app restart.**
Store is sessionStorage-backed (`store.ts:835`), so a real restart starts empty. Resync mints then immediately deletes the outstanding record (`resyncAgentStatus.ts:485-487`) and cold-start-seeds the green away (`:509-520`). Nothing reported, and the repair block can't fire (`:558`, `:631-635` both false). A later pane-open bails at `companionAlertSync.ts:395`. An unread green older than the host session boundary is orphaned on the phone for its TTL. Documented `(DOT-PERSIST)` tradeoff, but it is a clear-without-report.

**GAP R3 — workspace deletion (soft-delete to Recycle Bin).**
No renderer path reports on delete; the row just leaves the sidebar. Terminals then die → GAP R1.

**Latent:** `useMarkSidebarWorkspaceTerminalsSeen` (`DashboardSidebarWorkspaceStatusProvider.tsx:374-387`) clears green with the non-reporting variant. Currently has no callers.

---

## Third alert type — terminal-agent FAILURE (`e`)

**GAPPED.** `findReadAlerts` filters `alert.kind !== "g"` (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:1044`) and the blind fallback hard-codes `kind: "g"` (`:1268`). Desktop "seen" can never retract a failure alert. Only `session-end` or TTL takes it down. Explicitly intentional per the comment at `:118-120` ("an error the user has not seen is exactly the thing that must not be quietly folded away") — but the lived consequence is that opening the failed chat on the desktop leaves the phone and watch failure alert standing.

Note the asymmetry at `:1160-1173`: a `failed` on top of a PermissionRequest DOES mint an `e`, so a blocked agent that dies produces a failure alert with no seen-retraction.

---

## Lingering edge cases

**Watch, question resolution — no retry queue.** `pushResolved` is one-shot (`WatchBridge.kt:250-266`). `NoWatchPaired` (`WatchLink.kt:127`) or `Failed` (`:150`) → wrist card survives. `QuestionNotifier.post` sets **no** `setTimeoutAfter` (`QuestionNotifier.kt:123-131`), and `onHello` replays lifecycle dismissals but not resolutions (`WatchBridge.kt:546-562`). Card clears only on user tap or swipe — indefinitely otherwise. Sharpest asymmetry in the design: the lifecycle dismissal has a 24 h durable queue, the safety-critical question resolution has none.

**Watch, lifecycle — bounded.** `PendingLifecycleDismissals` (SharedPreferences, `commit()`, cap 256, TTL 24 h) queues before sending and clears only on clean delivery (`WatchBridge.kt:341-342`, `:381-385`). Replayed from `onHello` (`:553`) — i.e. when the watch app is FOREGROUNDED, not merely reconnected. A watch that is connected but whose app is never opened gets nothing until `setTimeoutAfter` expires the card.

**Phone offline at retraction time — questions OK.** Retraction shares the question's `collapse_key` (`PROTOCOL.md:2635-2636`), so a waking phone receives only the retraction; the cancel is a harmless no-op. I did **not** verify collapse behaviour for the lifecycle `c` frame — treat that as unchecked.

**Phone re-buzz after retraction.** `retract()` writes no negative record (contrast lifecycle's `SeenLifecycleEvents.suppress`). If a duplicate/late question push arrives after the retraction and the bridge is unreachable, `CompanionMessagingService.kt:344-364` posts a contentless card with no state check — a fresh buzz for an already-answered question. Cleared only by the foreground sweep.

**Foreground sweep covers questions ONLY.** `CompanionNotifications.sweep` filters `it.id == ID_QUESTION` (`CompanionNotifications.kt:396-406`), driven from `SessionViewModel.kt:183,216`. Lifecycle cards live under a different id precisely so the sweep can't see them (`:494-495`); their only backstop is `setTimeoutAfter` (`:271`).

**Lifecycle cancel has a structural hole.** A READY card is posted under tag `t:<terminalId>` but the two direct cancels address `e:<eventId>` and the raw eventId (`CompanionNotifications.kt:298,303`). The terminal-keyed card is reachable only via `forgetRetracted`'s return value; if the shown-alert record was evicted (cap 64, `ShownLifecycleAlerts.kt:196`) or aged out, it returns `null` (`:185`) and the ready card is not cancelled. Mirrored on the watch (`LifecycleNotifier.kt:187-202`). Also `forgetRetracted` checks terminalId before eventId (`:173`), so an ERROR retraction carrying a terminal that holds a live READY collaterally cancels the READY.

**Lost-contact notification** (`CompanionNotifications.kt:349-379`) has no FCM retraction at all, no sweep coverage, no `setTimeoutAfter`, no watch relay. By design — raised locally by the phone.

---

`lifecycleEvents.ts` and `V2WorkspaceView.tsx` are modified in the working tree, but the specific lines GAP R1 and the pane-change trigger rest on are committed and unchanged. Everything else cited (`store.ts`, `companionAlertSync.ts`, `resyncAgentStatus.ts`, both sidebar hooks, all of `packages/host-service/src/companion/*`) is clean.

FEATURES.md restates this ("exact-equality in both directions"). Every wire-level fix below therefore uses existing keys only.

Live now, independent of FIX-3.

**Ambiguity + assumption (FIX-3 blind fallback).** The user asked for `e` to retract on desktop-seen. I widen the live-map path (covers everything the host still holds) but keep the blind restart fallback `g`-only. Hashing both kinds doubles blind broadcasts on every warm reconnect, which is the exact harm documented at `lifecycle-alerts.ts:1225-1233` (the phone's 64-slot `SeenLifecycleEvents` window evicting real tombstones). Residual stated in §5. Say the word if the user wants both.

## 1. Recommended approach

### FIX-1 — durable watch question-retraction (COMP only, no wire change)

Clone the `PendingLifecycleDismissals` shape for resolutions, and give the wrist card a self-timeout.

* Durable queue `PendingQuestionResolutions` (SharedPreferences, `commit()`, cap 256, TTL 6 h), enqueue-before-send, clear on clean delivery, replay on `onHello` — byte-for-byte the same discipline as `COMP\app-phone\...\wear\PendingLifecycleDismissals.kt` and `WatchBridge.kt:335-387`.
* Persist only `questionId | enqueuedAtMs | resolvedAtMs | resolvedBySurface | outcome`. **`resolvedByLabel` is deliberately not persisted**: all three `pushResolved` callers pass `null` (`ConversationViewModel.kt:518-524`, `SessionViewModel.kt:231-237`, `CompanionMessagingService.kt:209-215`) and nothing on the wrist renders it (only `resolvedBySurface`, `app-watch\...\ui\AnswerStateScreen.kt:283`). Keeps the `:`/`,` delimiter encoding safe — every persisted token is base64url or a fixed enum word.
* TTL 6 h, not the dismissals' 24 h: it matches the question staleness horizon and the new wrist timeout, so a queued entry can never outlive the card it cancels. (24 h for symmetry is the alternative; 6 h is self-consistent, pick it.)
* Belt: `setTimeoutAfter` on the wrist question card, computed from `askedAtMs + QUESTION_STALE_AFTER_MS`. `QUESTION_STALE_AFTER_MS = 21_600_000` mirrors the bridge's `PUSH_QUESTION_EXPIRY_MS` (`packages\host-service\src\companion\config.ts:377`). Rejected alternative: carry the push's `x` to the watch on `WatchQuestion` — two of the three post paths (`pushQuestionById`, `onHello`) have no push in hand, so it would need the constant anyway; one mechanism beats two.

Why not "replay by re-fetching at hello" (call `pushQuestionById` for queued ids): it needs the desktop reachable at exactly the moment the watch reconnects, which is the case the queue exists for.

### FIX-2 — stuck ready alerts (host-side authority, FORK; one shared Kotlin fix, COMP)

Three producers, three answers, all authored where the fact is known:

* **R1 (terminal died)** — one choke point. Every exit broadcast in the tree funnels through `EventBus.broadcastTerminalLifecycle` (`packages\host-service\src\events\event-bus.ts:224`); the nine call sites are all in `terminal\terminal.ts` and `terminal\reaper\reaper.ts`. Hook a fork-only companion sink there, gated on `eventType === "exit" && confirmed !== false`, and have it call a new `LifecycleAlertManager.retireTerminal()` which is just `supersede(id, { kind: "session-end" })` — the existing, already-correct retirement. **No renderer change**, so nothing collides with the fixer's in-flight `lifecycleEvents.ts` edit.
  Rejected: calling from each of the 9 broadcast sites (silently incomplete the first time someone adds a tenth). Rejected: an `EventBus.onTerminalLifecycle` in-process listener like `onWorkspaceChanged` (`event-bus.ts:258-266`) — better dependency direction, but it needs the `EventBus` threaded into the bridge through both mounts (`serve.ts:134`, `apps\desktop\src\main\host-service\index.ts:154`) for no behavioural gain. The registry sink slot is what `registry.ts:253` exists for and answers "nobody consumed it" when the bridge is off.
  Rejected: polling `liveness.isProvablyGone` from the alert sweep — up to ~90 s late, silent when the daemon is unreachable, and the sweep only runs while a `held` alert exists (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:601-615`).

* **R2 (app restart)** — renderer supplies the one fact only it has, host decides what to retract. New one-shot mutation carrying the cold-start boundary the resync already computes in **host clock** terms (`resyncAgentStatus.ts:393-407`); the host retires `g` alerts with `outcomeAtMs < boundary`. One call per host per launch, zero blind broadcasts, no per-terminal `reportTerminalSeen` storm — which is what `companionAlertSync.ts:419-423` and `lifecycle-alerts.ts:1225-1233` explicitly forbid.
  Rejected: inferring the cold start host-side from `sidebar_mirror_meta.appLaunchId`. `APP_LAUNCH_ID` is `crypto.randomUUID()` at module scope (`...CollectionsProvider\dashboardSidebarLocal\sidebarVisibility.ts:73`) so it changes on a renderer **reload**, where sessionStorage survives and the desktop keeps its green dot — the host would retract a card the user has not read. Wrong proxy.
  Rejected: renderer reporting seen for every cold-seeded terminal — the documented mass-retraction the design forbids.

* **R3 (soft delete / any curation-off)** — the mirror is the authority and already reaches the bridge. On a mirror change, retire live alerts whose workspace is now curated off. The hold rule already exists (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:639`, `createLifecycleCurationProbe`); this applies the same rule to the states the hold cannot reach (`sent`/`standing`). Hook: the existing `mirrorChangeSink` at `packages\host-service\src\companion\index.ts:1222-1239`.

* **Phone addressing (shared with FIX-3, MUST)** — `forgetRetracted` returns a key or `null`, and `null` conflates "no record" with "stale retraction" (`ShownLifecycleAlerts.kt:171-189`). Cap-64 eviction (`:142`) or an expired row therefore leaves the terminal-keyed card uncancellable. Make the verdict three-valued and cancel the terminal key on **Unknown** only — an unknown record is ignorance, and a stuck card is worse than one extra cancel (the next finish reposts and buzzes). Keep the generation guard on **Stale**, which is the whole point of `gx`.

### FIX-3 — error alerts retract on desktop-seen (FORK + COMP + PROTOCOL)

* Drop the `kind !== "g"` filter in `findReadAlerts` (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:1044`). `notifiesPhone` already returns true for `e` + `seen`, so nothing else in the state machine changes.
* Keep the blind fallback `g`-only (`:1264-1274`), documented (see §0).
* **`fireRetraction` sends `t: ""` for `kind === "e"`.** This is the precise-addressing fix, and it is wire-legal today: PROTOCOL §13.5.1 already says `t` "may be `""` on `q`, `e` and `c`", and both `sanitizeTerminalHandle` (`push.ts:771-775`) and the phone parser (`LifecyclePush.kt:241,247`) accept empty. An `e` card is always event-keyed on both devices (`LifecycleAlertIdentity.key`, `ShownLifecycleAlerts.kt:30-35`), so an `e` retraction has no terminal-collapsed row to name — and sending one is the pre-existing collateral bug in §0.
* Record the user's override in the `notifiesPhone` doc (`:118-120`) and in `markLifecycleSeen`'s "ONLY `g`" note (`:1197-1201`), dated 2026-08-20.
