## 3. Data flow

**FIX-1, resolution:** phone resolves (answer confirmed / sweep / FCM retract) → `WatchBridge.pushResolved` → **enqueue first** (durable, `commit()`) → `WatchLink.sendResolved` → `Delivered && failedNodeCount==0` → `clear(questionId)`. `NoWatchPaired`/`Failed`/partial → row stays. Watch reconnects → `HELLO` → `onHello` → `flushLifecycleDismissals` → `flushQuestionResolutions` → oldest pending question. Watch: `QUESTION_RESOLVED` → `QuestionNotifier.cancel` + `QuestionStore.onResolved` (idempotent for unknown ids, `QuestionStore.kt:327-346`). Independently, every wrist question card self-cancels at `askedAtMs + 6 h`. Validation: at the queue boundary (`enqueue` refuses over cap and reports `SentUnrecorded`, never silently drops) and at `WatchLink.send` (`MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` require).

**FIX-2 R1:** pty exit / dispose / reaper correction → `EventBus.broadcastTerminalLifecycle({eventType:"exit", confirmed?})` → fork hook, `confirmed !== false` → `recordCompanionTerminalGone` → (bridge off ⇒ `false`, no-op) → `lifecycleAlerts.retireTerminal` → `supersede(terminalId, {kind:"session-end"})` → per alert: `held` deleted silently, `sending` flagged (retracted on `markDelivered`), `sent`/`standing` → one `c` each, `retracted` inert. Phone: `routeLifecycleRetract` → suppress tombstone first, then cancel, then watch dismissal (`LifecycleRouting.kt:278-289`). State persisted: none new on the host (alert table stays process-local, per `lifecycle-alerts.ts:416-421`); durable state is the phone's `ShownLifecycleAlerts` + suppression gate.

**FIX-2 R2:** desktop cold start → renderer resync computes `hostSessionBoundary = hostNow - elapsedMonotonic` → `companion.retireStaleReadyAlerts({boundaryMs})` (validated at the tRPC boundary: positive int; the sink additionally refuses `boundaryMs > now()` — a future boundary would retire everything) → `retireReadyBefore` → `retireAlert(..., {kind:"seen"})` per covered `g`. Same instant the renderer seeds those terminals as seen locally (`resyncAgentStatus.ts:509-520`), so both surfaces agree.

**FIX-2 R3:** user bins/archives/snoozes/completes a thread → `useSidebarMirrorSync` full-replace → `sidebarMirror.sync` → `replaceMirror` (`sidebar-mirror.ts:186-260`, content hash decides `changed`) → `publishCompanionMirrorChanged` → `mirrorChangeSink` → `tree.curation` event **and** `retireCuratedOffAlerts()` → `workspaceSidebarVerdict !== "show"` → one `c` per live alert. DB (`sidebar_workspace_state`) is the source of truth for the verdict; the probe's 30 s positive cache is fine here because a cached `true` is the state we act on.

**FIX-3:** user opens the chat on the desktop → `markTerminalSeenAndReportRead` (`companionAlertSync.ts:371-413`) → `companion.markLifecycleSeen` → bridge revalidates terminal↔workspace against host.db (`index.ts:917-953`) → `findReadAlerts` now returns `g` **and** `e` rows with `outcomeAtMs <= seenThroughAt` → `retireAlert(..., {kind:"seen"})` → `fireRetraction` with `t = handle` for `g`, `t = ""` for `e` → phone: `e` cancels its event-keyed row only; `g` cancels event key + terminal key under the generation guard.

---

## 4. Build order

Each step independently verifiable. **Fork work lands as its own commit after the master-workspace feature; companion work is independent and can land first.**

**COMP (independent, ship first — it makes the fork's retractions land correctly on shipped phones):**
1. `ShownLifecycleAlerts` verdict + both cancel sites + tests. Verify: `gradlew :core:test :app-phone:test :app-watch:test`.
2. `QUESTION_STALE_AFTER_MS` + wrist question `setTimeoutAfter` + PROTOCOL §15 row. Verify: unit test on the computed timeout; manual on-wrist check that a card self-clears.
3. `PendingQuestionResolutions` + store + tests (pure JVM, no device).
4. `WatchBridge.pushResolved`/`flushQuestionResolutions`/`onHello` + container wiring + `WatchBridgeIntentTest` update.
5. PROTOCOL §13.5.5/§13.5.6 paragraphs.

**FORK (after the master-workspace commit is in):**
6. `lifecycle-alerts.ts` alone: `findReadAlerts` widening, `fireRetraction` empty-`t`-for-`e`, the three new manager methods, the `curated-off` reason, comment updates. Verify: `bun test packages/host-service/src/companion/lifecycle-alerts.test.ts` + `push-retract.test.ts`. Nothing is wired yet, so this step cannot change runtime behaviour beyond FIX-3.
7. `registry.ts` slots + `index.ts` registration + `mirrorChangeSink` call (R3 live). Verify: bun tests; manual — bin a thread with a ready card on the phone, card disappears.
8. `event-bus.ts` hook + FEATURES.md marker (R1 live). Verify: `bun run scripts/check-feature-markers.mjs`; manual — kill a terminal with a ready card, card disappears.
9. tRPC mutation + renderer `reportColdStartBoundary` + resync call (R2 live). Verify: renderer bun tests; manual — leave a ready card, quit and relaunch the desktop, card disappears.
10. FEATURES.md manifest prose. `/simplify` before commit (production code touched).

## 5. Risks and edge cases

* **Marker survival.** The `event-bus.ts` hook is fork-only code in an upstream-shaped file. Without the FEATURES.md marker row a nightly merge can take upstream's version and every gate stays green while R1 silently dies. Same failure class the fork already wrote `(COMPANION-CAPTURE-HOOK)` for.
* **`confirmed: false` exits.** Deliberately not retired — the row stays `active` for the reaper, which emits the confirming exit (`terminal.ts:2905-2915`, `reaper.ts:472-479`). Cost: a card lives until the reaper's pass (observed up to 21 min in the DISPOSE-LIMBO note). Retiring on the unconfirmed exit would retract a card for a terminal that may still be alive.
* **Host-service restart (residual, R2).** Alert state is process-local by design. Desktop restart + host restart ⇒ nothing holds the alert, nothing can name it, and the card lives to its 6 h TTL — the phone's `setTimeoutAfter` (`CompanionNotifications.kt:271`) takes it down, no manual swipe. The only cure is either durable alert state (schema change, needs explicit user approval) or a phone-side lifecycle sweep against the tree — both out of scope.
* **`e` retraction after a host restart (residual, FIX-3).** Blind fallback stays `g`-only, so a read does not retract a restart-orphaned `e`. Dies at TTL.
* **Snooze retracts too (R3).** `curated-off` covers snooze, not just the bin. Defensible ("not now" ⇒ take the buzz away), but it is a behaviour change beyond the literal ask — flag to the user. If unwanted, gate `retireCuratedOffAlerts` on the verdict being `deleted`/`completed`/`archived` only; `workspaceSidebarVerdict` already returns the discriminated reason (`sidebar-filter.ts:95-102`), so the narrowing is one condition.
* **Cancel-on-Unknown (phone).** Trades a possible extra cancel of an unrecorded newer card for never stranding one. The next finish reposts and buzzes, so the worst case is one missed buzz on an unread chat whose record was evicted at cap 64 — the same trade PROTOCOL §13.5.6 already documents ("one extra buzz, never a stuck notification").
* **Retraction storms.** `retireCuratedOffAlerts` runs on every *changed* mirror sync, `retireReadyBefore` once per host per launch. Both are bounded by the alert table (512) and every path goes through `retireAlert`, whose `retracted` rows are inert — no alert can fire two `c`s.
* **Clock.** `boundaryMs` is host clock by construction (`hostNow - elapsedMonotonic`); a renderer that could not read `performance.now()` or `hostNow` skips cold-start seeding entirely (`resyncAgentStatus.ts:394-405`) and must skip the report too — same guard, do not report a boundary the seeding did not use.
* **Concurrency (phone).** `PendingQuestionResolutions` must be `@Synchronized` on the instance and constructed **once** in the container, for the reason `LifecycleNotifier` spells out — the Data Layer dispatches on a pool and a per-call instance is no lock at all.
* **Migration.** The new SharedPreferences file starts empty; a resolution sent by the old build and lost stays lost (bounded by the new wrist timeout once the watch updates). No fork DB schema change anywhere in this design.
* **Rollout order (PROTOCOL §13.5.7 is a hard gate).** The empty-`t`-on-`e` change is safe in either order — every shipped phone already accepts `t: ""` on `c`. The phone's verdict change is a pure improvement. So there is no coupled rollout, which is why the two commits can land independently.
* **Overlap with the in-flight master-workspace work.** `lifecycleEvents.ts`, `events/types.ts`, `terminal/terminal.ts` and `eventBus.ts` (workspace-client) are all dirty right now. This design touches **none** of them except `packages/host-service/src/events/event-bus.ts` (host, not the workspace-client one) — check for a conflict there at rebase time, and re-read `broadcastTerminalLifecycle` after the `created` event lands, since the new `created` variant flows through the same method and must fall through the hook's `eventType === "exit"` guard.

---

## 6. Out of scope

* Durable host-side alert state (would need a `host.db` schema change and explicit user approval).
* Phone-side reconciliation of *lifecycle* cards against the tree on foreground (`CompanionNotifications.sweep` stays questions-only). This is the only thing that would close the double-restart residual.
* Question-path retraction (`v1 r`), the answer path, leases, pairing — untouched.
* Any change to the `q`/`g`/`e` push key sets, to `collapse_key`, or to the dedupe/suppression window.
* The `new-cycle` silent-retirement rule for `g` — unchanged.
* Watch UI beyond the notification timeout.
* Kanban / sidebar / recycle-bin behaviour: R3 reads the mirror, it never writes curation.

</agent-message>

## 0. Decisions carried in from review, and one new finding

**Confirmed pre-existing bug (both reviewers).** `fireRetraction` passes a real terminal handle for every kind (`packages\host-service\src\companion\lifecycle-alerts.ts:665`); the phone reads a non-empty `t` on a `c` as "retire the terminal-collapsed READY row up to `gx`" (`COMP\core\...\notify\ShownLifecycleAlerts.kt:173-181`); `notifiesPhone` fires for `e` + `new-cycle` (`:122-124`). So an error followed by fresh work silently cancels the user's unread ready card, today, with no part of this feature enabled. Fixed by §FIX-3's empty-`t` rule.

**New finding — M4's hop is exact, not a hash guess.** `notifications.ts:307-318` computes `occurredAt = nextLifecycleInstantMs(...)` **once** and passes the same value to `broadcastAgentLifecycle` (`:317`) and to `forwardCompanionLifecycle` (`:341`). So the renderer's `payload.occurredAt` for a `Stop`/`Failed` event **is** the `outcomeAtMs` the alert id hashes. The visible-clear report can therefore use `seenThroughAt = payload.occurredAt` and land on the live map every time — no blind broadcast, no `canProveAbsence` gamble.

**M4 scope, pinned.** Only `eventType === "Stop"` and `"Failed"` can mint an alert: `apps\desktop\src\main\lib\agent-jsonl-watcher\pane-map-hook.ts:2981-2985` sets `lifecycle_outcome` to `"failed"` / `"ready"` for exactly those, and `lifecycle-alerts.ts:1153` ignores every other outcome. The report must be scoped to those two event types — **not** to "any event reaching the `statusTransitions.ts:98` tail", because `BackgroundRunning` reaches that tail too and mints nothing.

**Intentional asymmetry, stated so nobody "fixes" it.** R2 (desktop relaunch) retires `g` only. FIX-3 retracts `e` on a real read of that terminal; a cold relaunch is not a read of anything — the renderer's cold-start seeding is a heuristic about idle greens, and silently dropping an unseen agent-death on app restart is the failure this alert class exists to prevent.

**Fail-direction split, stated so nobody unifies it.** The curation HOLD fails open (uncertain ⇒ fire the alert, `lifecycle-alerts.ts:288-295`). The curation RETIRE fails closed (uncertain ⇒ retire nothing). Both fail toward the user being told.

## 1. FORK — host-service

### 1.1 `packages\host-service\src\companion\lifecycle-alerts.ts` (MUST)

**Reasons.** Add three `RetireReason` members: `{kind:"terminal-gone"}`, `{kind:"desktop-relaunch"}`, `{kind:"curated-off"}`. `retireReasonRank:79-81` needs **no edit** — it returns 1 for everything that is not `new-cycle`, so all three are loud and can upgrade a queued `new-cycle` on a `sending` row. Distinct names rather than reusing `session-end` so `fireRetraction`'s log line (`:654-659`) says which mechanism fired. **Test-pin the rank and the `notifiesPhone` verdict for all three** (review pt 9).

**`notifiesPhone:122-124`** — no code change. Update the doc block: `:118-120`'s "`e` ALERTS ARE UNCHANGED" paragraph now records the user's override — desktop-seen retracts an `e`, decided 2026-08-20 — while the silent case remains exactly `g` + `new-cycle`.

**`findReadAlerts:1037-1049`** — delete the `if (alert.kind !== "g") continue;` at `:1044`. Nothing else in the function changes; the `outcomeAtMs > seenThroughAt` boundary and the newest-first sort already do the right thing for both kinds.

**`markLifecycleSeen:1176-1275`** — the blind fallback at `:1264-1274` stays `kind: "g"`. Rewrite the "ONLY `g`" comment at `:1197-1201` to say what is now true: the live map covers both kinds; the blind restart hash stays ready-only because hashing both would double the blind broadcasts the `:1225-1233` note exists to avoid, and an `e`'s id is only recomputable when `seenThroughAt` coincides exactly with its outcome instant. Residual in §6.

**`fireRetraction:653-680`** — `terminalHandle: alert.kind === "g" ? deps.terminalHandle(alert.hostTerminalId) : ""`. `gx` is **unchanged and still required** — `buildLifecycleRetractPushData:984-988` refuses a non-positive `outcomeAtMs`, and `e` rows carry a real one from `mint:1068`. **Test-pin that an `e` retraction has `t === "" && gx === String(outcomeAtMs)`** (review pt 15).

**Three new manager methods** on `LifecycleAlertManager` (`:203-212`):

```ts
retireTerminal(hostTerminalId: string): void;
retireReadyBefore(boundaryMs: number): number;   // returns alerts retired
retireCuratedOffAlerts(isCuratedOffFresh: (hostWorkspaceId: string) => boolean): number;
```

* `retireTerminal` → `supersede(hostTerminalId, { kind: "terminal-gone" })`. Nothing else; `supersede:878-883` + `retireAlert:895-938` already handle all five states correctly.
* `retireReadyBefore` → walk `alerts`, take `kind === "g" && outcomeAtMs < boundaryMs`, `retireAlert(alert, { kind: "desktop-relaunch" })`. **Includes `held`** (deleted silently, no `c`) — the evidence is "the desktop has seeded these as read", which retires a never-sent alert too.
* `retireCuratedOffAlerts` — **takes the fresh probe as a parameter** (review pt 1). It must never reach for `deps.isCuratedOff`, whose 30-second positive cache (`:277-279`) would retire alerts for a workspace whose snooze expired inside the window. Signature makes that impossible to get wrong and is test-injectable.

**`retireCuratedOffAlerts` algorithm** (review pt 2 — flip-scoped, held-preserving):

1. Collect the distinct `hostWorkspaceId`s of alerts in state `sending | sent | standing` only. **`held` is excluded**: it is still under the existing fire-at-expiry hold at `claim:639`, and deleting it would drop a mid-snooze finish the user has never seen.
2. For each, call `isCuratedOffFresh`. Compare against a manager-owned `Map<string, boolean> curationVerdictByWorkspace`.
3. Retire that workspace's `sending|sent|standing` alerts **only on a false→true flip**, with `{kind:"curated-off"}`. An unchanged `true` retires nothing — otherwise every unrelated mirror write (the 5-minute heartbeat included) would re-walk and re-log.
4. Record the new verdict either way. Prune map entries for workspaces with no live alerts, so it stays bounded by the alert table.
5. A workspace absent from the map on its first sighting seeds as `false` **before** the flip test, so a workspace that was already curated off when its alert was minted does not get retired by the first sync it appears in. (The mint path could not have produced it anyway — `claim:639` holds — but state it.)

Net effect, which is exactly what the user approved: snooze/bin/archive/complete takes the standing card off the phone now, and a finish that happens *during* the snooze still fires when the snooze lapses.

**Scope note.** `curated-off` covers snooze, not just the bin. If the user wants bin/archive/complete only, `workspaceSidebarVerdict` already returns the discriminated reason (`sidebar-filter.ts:95-102`) — one condition in the `index.ts` closure narrows it, no change here.

## 2. FORK — renderer

### 2.1 `...\V2NotificationController\lib\companionAlertSync.ts` (MUST)

New export, same silent-on-failure contract as `reportTerminalSeen:435-468`, keyed by `hostUrl` directly (the caller has it; `hostUrlByWorkspaceId` is workspace-keyed and irrelevant here):

```ts
export function reportRelaunchBoundary({ hostUrl, boundaryMs }): Promise<boolean>
```

Guard `Number.isSafeInteger(boundaryMs) && boundaryMs > 0` before sending; resolve `false` otherwise. Never rejects.

**Acknowledged-latch** (review pt 7): module-level `const relaunchBoundaryReportedHosts = new Set<string>()`, added **only** on `accepted === true`. A transport failure or `accepted: false` leaves the host eligible on the next resync — `retireReadyBefore` is idempotent (a `retracted` row is inert, `retireAlert:896`), so retries are free. Deliberately **not** cleared by `forgetAlertContextSyncsForHost:287-298`: a host reconnect is not a new desktop launch, and the set's lifetime is the renderer's, which is the launch.

### 2.2 `...\V2NotificationController\lib\resyncAgentStatus.ts` (MUST)

At `:398-401`, where `hostSessionBoundaries.set(hostUrl, hostNow - elapsedMs)` runs — that map already dedupes per host (`:393`), so this is once per host per cold start:

* Keep the **fractional** value in the map (local comparisons at `:512` are unchanged).
* Report `Math.floor(hostNow - elapsedMs)` (review pt 4). Flooring biases the boundary **earlier**, so it can only ever retire strictly fewer alerts — the safe direction, same bias the cold-start seeding already has.
* Fire-and-forget through `reportRelaunchBoundary`; on `accepted`, add to the latch set.
* Both existing bail-outs (`:394-397` no monotonic clock, `:401-405` no `hostNow`) must also skip the report — if the seeding did not happen, its boundary must not be published.
* Add `relaunchBoundaryReported: boolean` to `ResyncResult` (`:409-424`) for the existing diagnostic log.

### 2.3 `...\V2NotificationController\lib\lifecycleEvents.ts` (MUST — this is the M4 hop)

**Nothing at `:187-203`.** R1 is host-side; the terminal-exit block stays exactly as the in-flight master-workspace commit leaves it.

In `updatePaneStatus`, immediately after `clearSources(workspaceId, transition.clearSources)` at `:278`:

```ts
// (ALERT-CONTEXT-NAMES) The user was LOOKING at the pane when the turn ended.
if (
    targetVisible &&
    (payload.eventType === "Stop" || payload.eventType === "Failed") &&
    transition.axes === null &&
    target.terminalId.length > 0
) {
    useV2NotificationStore.getState().markTerminalSeen(target.terminalId, payload.occurredAt);
    void reportTerminalSeen({
        workspaceId,
        terminalId: target.terminalId,
        seenThroughAt: payload.occurredAt,
    });
}
```

Why each clause:

* `targetVisible` — the whole point. The `wasAwaitingPermission && !targetVisible` case takes the same `:101-102` clear path but the user is **not** looking, so it must not report.
* the two event types — only these mint alerts (§0). `transition.axes === null` asserts the clear path actually ran rather than re-deriving `statusTransitions`' logic.
* `payload.occurredAt` — exactly the `outcomeAtMs` the alert id hashes (§0). This is what makes the hop land on the live map.
* `markTerminalSeen` locally as well: reporting "seen" to the host while the renderer keeps no watermark is the divergence that makes the next resync re-raise a green for an event the user watched, and then re-report it. `markTerminalSeen` only bumps forward (`store.ts:365-366`) and instants are monotonic per terminal, so a **later** finish still raises its review normally.

**No debounce, deliberately.** Idempotence is structural: one report per event, keyed by that event's own instant; a duplicate WS delivery produces an identical `seenThroughAt`; `retireAlert` is idempotent and the tombstone path (`retractById:955-992`) dedupes repeats. `targetVisible` is per-target, so a burst across terminals cannot fan out. Adding a timer here would only delay the report past the alert it retracts.

**Not `markTerminalSeenAndReportRead`.** Its guard `if (!clearedReview && outstandingAt === null) return` (`companionAlertSync.ts:395`) refuses precisely this case — there is no review entry and no outstanding record, by construction. Call `reportTerminalSeen` directly.

**Ordering note for the implementer.** In the common case the alert never exists: a visible pane means the user is present, presence gating holds the alert at `claim:638`, and the read deletes the held row silently. The report matters when the window is visible but unfocused and presence has lapsed. The renderer can never beat the mint — the router mints synchronously at `:341`, before the renderer's round trip.

## 3. COMP — phone / watch / core

### 3.1 `app-phone\...\phone\wear\PendingQuestionResolutions.kt` (NEW, MUST)

Structural clone of `PendingLifecycleDismissals.kt`: `PendingQuestionResolution` data class, `PendingResolutionStore` interface, `PendingQuestionResolutions(store, capacity = 256, ttlMs = 21_600_000)`, `SharedPreferencesPendingResolutionStore` (own file `companion_question_resolutions`, `commit()`, same `,`/`:` encoding, same `@Synchronized`, same "a full queue refuses loudly and drops nothing" rule).

Persisted fields: `questionId | enqueuedAtMs | resolvedAtMs | resolvedBySurface | outcome`. `resolvedAtMs` is nullable — encode absent as an empty field, decode empty as `null`. Delimiter-safe: `questionId` is 22-char base64url, the other two are fixed enum tokens.

**`resolvedByLabel` is not persisted.** All three `pushResolved` callers pass `null` (`ConversationViewModel.kt:518-524`, `SessionViewModel.kt:231-237`, `CompanionMessagingService.kt:209-215`) and nothing on the wrist renders it (`app-watch\...\ui\AnswerStateScreen.kt:283` reads only `resolvedBySurface`). The two `resolvedMessage(...)` paths (§3.2) *can* carry a label: the **immediate send keeps it**, only a **replay** degrades it to `null`. Document that in the data class.

TTL 6 h, not the dismissals' 24 h: it equals the question staleness horizon and the new wrist timeout, so a queued entry can never outlive the card it would cancel.

* `onHello:546-562` — call it **after** `flushLifecycleDismissals` and **before** the oldest-pending-question push, so a queued resolution can never cancel the card hello just posted.

### 3.3 `app-phone\...\phone\di\CompanionContainer.kt` (MUST)

New `by lazy` for `pendingQuestionResolutions` beside `:213-214`; pass at `:222`. **One instance for the process** — `@Synchronized` locks the instance, and the Data Layer dispatches on a pool (the reasoning `LifecycleNotifier` already spells out for its own singleton).

### 3.4 `core\...\core\notify\ShownLifecycleAlerts.kt` (MUST — serves FIX-2 and FIX-3)

`forgetRetracted:165-189` returns a three-valued verdict instead of `String?`:

```kotlin
sealed interface RetractionVerdict {
    data class Retired(val key: String) : RetractionVerdict   // record removed
    data object Stale : RetractionVerdict                     // record outranks this retraction
    data object Unknown : RetractionVerdict                   // no record at all
}
```

Mapping from today's behaviour: the `held.generationAtMs > generationAtMs` early return at `:177` becomes `Stale`; a removal becomes `Retired(key)`; both "no terminal row" **and** "no event row" become `Unknown` (keeping the `:184` compaction write). Logic otherwise unchanged.

### 3.5 `app-phone\...\phone\notifications\CompanionNotifications.kt` + `app-watch\...\wear\notify\LifecycleNotifier.kt` (MUST)

`cancelLifecycle:297-311` and `LifecycleNotifier.cancel` consume the verdict identically — one rule, both surfaces, as PROTOCOL §13.5.6 requires:

* `Retired(key)` → cancel that key (today's behaviour).
* `Stale` → cancel nothing beyond the unconditional event-key cancels. The generation guard is the whole point of `gx`.
* `Unknown` **and** `terminalId` non-empty → cancel `terminalKey(terminalId)` anyway.

The `Unknown` branch is the cap-64 / expired-record hole: `record:142` keeps only the last 63 rows, so a busy phone loses the record while the card is still on screen and the terminal-keyed card becomes uncancellable. Trade: at worst one extra cancel of a card whose record was evicted, and the next finish reposts and buzzes — the same "one extra buzz, never a stuck notification" trade PROTOCOL §13.5.6 already documents. **Test both directions: `Stale` never cancels, `Unknown` cancels** (review pt 14).

### 3.6 `app-watch\...\wear\notify\QuestionNotifier.kt` (MUST)

`post:89-135` gains `timeoutAtMs: Long`; add to the builder, **as a relative duration** (review pt 6), matching `LifecycleNotifier.kt:151`:

```kotlin
.setTimeoutAfter((timeoutAtMs - System.currentTimeMillis()).coerceAtLeast(1))
```

* `notifyQuestion` → `question.askedAtMs + QUESTION_STALE_AFTER_MS`.
* `notifyOversize` / `notifyContentless` → `System.currentTimeMillis() + QUESTION_STALE_AFTER_MS`; neither payload carries `askedAtMs`, and arrival is a lower bound on the ask, so the card can never time out early.

### 3.7 `core\...\core\CompanionConstants.kt` (MUST)

`const val QUESTION_STALE_AFTER_MS = 21_600_000L` in a new "Question freshness" block. Must equal `PUSH_QUESTION_EXPIRY_MS` (`FORK\packages\host-service\src\companion\config.ts:377`). Cross-repo, so no test can bind the pair — put a comment naming the other file **on both sides**.

### 3.8 `COMP\PROTOCOL.md` (MUST)

* §15 constants table: `Question staleness horizon | 21 600 000 ms`.
* §13.5.6: an error retraction carries `t: ""`. An `e` card is event-keyed on both devices (`LifecycleAlertIdentity.key`), so it has no terminal-collapsed row to name, and a non-empty `t` retires a READY row the retraction was never about. Already legal — §13.5.1's table says `t` "may be `""` on `q`, `e` and `c`", and both `sanitizeTerminalHandle` (`push.ts:771-775`) and `LifecyclePush.kt:241,247` accept empty. **The `c` key set is unchanged**; `gx` stays required.
* §13.5.6: the three-valued retraction verdict and the cancel-on-`Unknown` rule.
* §13.5.5 / §11: question resolutions are eventual, not one-shot — same durable-queue wording as the watch dismissal, TTL 6 h, replay on `hello`, label degrades to absent on replay. And: the wrist question card carries its own `setTimeoutAfter` from `askedAtMs` + the horizon, because the watch has no equivalent of the phone's foreground `sweep`.

---

## 4. Data flow

**FIX-1.** Any resolution (answer confirmed / foreground sweep / FCM retract / `pushQuestionById` / `onAnswerIntent` stale-or-resolved reply) → `deliverResolution` → enqueue durably (`commit()`) → `WatchLink.sendResolved` → clean delivery clears the row; `NoWatchPaired`/`Failed`/partial leaves it. Watch reconnects → `HELLO` → `flushLifecycleDismissals` → `flushQuestionResolutions` → oldest pending question. Watch: `QUESTION_RESOLVED` → `QuestionNotifier.cancel` + `QuestionStore.onResolved` (idempotent for unknown ids, `QuestionStore.kt:327-346`). Independently, every wrist question card self-cancels at `askedAtMs + 6 h`.

**FIX-2 R1.** pty exit / dispose / reaper correction → `broadcastTerminalLifecycle` → guard → `recordCompanionTerminalGone` (bridge off ⇒ `false`, no-op) → `retireTerminal` → `supersede(..., terminal-gone)` → per alert: `held` deleted silently, `sending` flagged and retracted on `markDelivered:697-722`, `sent`/`standing` one `c` each, `retracted` inert → phone `routeLifecycleRetract` (`LifecycleRouting.kt:278-289`): suppress tombstone **first**, then cancel, then watch dismissal.

**FIX-2 R2.** Cold desktop start → resync computes `hostNow - elapsedMonotonic` → floor → `companion.retireStaleReadyAlerts` → tRPC validates positive finite int → sink refuses `> now()` → `retireReadyBefore` → `g` alerts with `outcomeAtMs < boundary` retired as `desktop-relaunch`. Latched per host on `accepted` only.

**FIX-2 R3.** Bin/archive/snooze/complete → `useSidebarMirrorSync` full replace → `sidebarMirror.sync` → `replaceMirror:186-260` (content hash decides `changed`; the hash covers `appLaunchId`, so a cold launch always counts as changed) → `publishCompanionMirrorChanged` → `mirrorChangeSink` → `tree.curation` publish, then, separately guarded, `retireCuratedOffAlerts(fresh probe)` → flip-scoped retirement of `sending|sent|standing` only. `sidebar_workspace_state` is the source of truth for the verdict.

**FIX-2 phone addressing.** Every `c` above → `cancelLifecycle` → unconditional event-key cancel + legacy raw-id cancel → verdict → `Retired`/`Unknown` cancel the terminal key, `Stale` does not.

**FIX-3, two entry points.** (a) User opens the chat → `markTerminalSeenAndReportRead:371-413` → `companion.markLifecycleSeen` → bridge revalidates terminal↔workspace against host.db (`index.ts:917-953`) → widened `findReadAlerts` returns `g` **and** `e` → `fireRetraction` with `t = handle` for `g`, `t = ""` for `e`. (b) Turn ends while the pane is visible → `updatePaneStatus` hop → `reportTerminalSeen(seenThroughAt = payload.occurredAt)` → same path, exact id match.

Validation boundaries: tRPC input schemas (both mutations); the bridge's host.db revalidation for the seen signal; the relaunch sink's `boundaryMs <= now`; `assertLifecycleIdentity` + `sanitizeTerminalHandle` when the frame is built; `requireExactKeySet` on the phone. Persisted state: no new host DB schema anywhere. New durable client state: one SharedPreferences file on the phone.

---

---

## 6. Documented residuals

1. **Unconfirmed dispose delay.** `confirmed: false` exits are deliberately not retired; the reaper's confirming pass is what fires the retraction — observed up to 21 minutes in the DISPOSE-LIMBO note at `terminal.ts:2884-2892`. Retiring on the unconfirmed exit would retract a card for a terminal that may still be running.
2. **Double restart.** Alert state is process-local by design (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:416-421`). Desktop **and** host-service both restarting leaves no row to name, so nothing retracts and the card lives to its 6 h TTL — taken down by the phone's own `setTimeoutAfter` (`CompanionNotifications.kt:271`), never needing a manual swipe. Cures: durable alert state (host DB schema change, explicit user approval required) or a phone-side lifecycle sweep. Both out of scope.
3. **`e` after a host restart.** The blind fallback stays `g`-only, so a read does not retract a restart-orphaned error alert; it dies at TTL. Reversible in one line if the user prefers the doubled blind broadcasts.
4. **Cap-64 eviction.** Cancel-on-`Unknown` closes the stuck-card case at the cost of a possible extra cancel of an unrecorded newer card; bounded to one missed buzz, re-raised by the next finish.
5. **Snooze retracts.** `curated-off` includes snooze. Behaviour change beyond the literal ask, one condition to narrow.
6. **Cross-repo constant.** `QUESTION_STALE_AFTER_MS` and `PUSH_QUESTION_EXPIRY_MS` cannot be bound by a test. Comments on both sides are the only guard.
7. **Merge exposure.** The `event-bus.ts` hook is fork-only code in an upstream-shaped file; the marker line is what makes its removal a red nightly rather than a silent regression.

## 7. Out of scope

Durable host-side alert state; phone-side lifecycle sweep against the tree (`CompanionNotifications.sweep` stays questions-only); the `v1 r` question-retraction path; the answer path, leases, pairing; any change to the `q`/`g`/`e`/`c` key sets, `collapse_key`, or the dedupe/suppression window; the `new-cycle` silent-retirement rule; watch UI beyond the notification timeout; the `BackgroundRunning`-sets-review oddity at `statusTransitions.ts:105-112` (noticed, pre-existing, unrelated).

</agent-message>

EMPTY-t VERIFICATION (the "shipped phones must already handle it" ask): they do. `t: ""` skips the terminal branch in forgetRetracted entirely and falls through to exact-event identity — which is the rule an error was always retired on — and the unconditional `cancel(eventKey(eventId))` covers the card regardless. DEVIATIONS (5, all justified):

1. **Cancel rule extracted to :core.** Spec 2 wanted both cancel sites to consume the verdict identically and tested BOTH directions. Both sites are Android-only (`NotificationManager`), and this project has no Robolectric and no mockk — JUnit + coroutines-test only — so a `when` duplicated at each site is untestable at either. Added `fun retractionCancelKey(verdict, terminalId): String?` to ShownLifecycleAlerts.kt: literally one rule, both surfaces, each site now a one-liner addressing its own id space (string tag / hashed int). Exhaustive `when` still breaks the build on a new verdict, in one place. Both directions tested: `Stale never cancels the terminal key`, `Unknown cancels the terminal key anyway`, plus an end-to-end capacity-eviction test.

2. **QuestionNotifier arithmetic behind two internal helpers.** `post` is private, so the spec's inline `.setTimeoutAfter((timeoutAtMs - System.currentTimeMillis()).coerceAtLeast(1))` could not also be tested as spec'd. Same arithmetic, now in top-level `internal questionStaleAtMs(fromMs)` and `questionTimeoutAfterMs(timeoutAtMs, nowMs)` (the seam `intentShapeError`/`draftFor` already use). Builder calls `questionTimeoutAfterMs(timeoutAtMs, System.currentTimeMillis())` — still relative, still floored at 1.

3. **`deliverResolution` returns `ResolutionDelivery(send, recorded)`, not bare `WatchSendResult`.** Forced: spec put the enqueue INSIDE deliverResolution (right call — three senders, not one), but pushResolved must report SentUnrecorded from the enqueue verdict, and a bare WatchSendResult throws it away. Body delegates to top-level `internal suspend deliverResolutionThrough(message, nowMs, queue, send)` — `WatchLink` is a concrete class taking a `Context`, not an interface, so this is the only way to test the enqueue-before-send ordering off device.

4. **No "WatchBridgeIntentTest construction update".** That premise is wrong — WatchBridgeIntentTest never constructs a WatchBridge; it drives top-level internal functions precisely because WatchLink is un-fakeable. Nothing to update; resolution coverage went into the new WatchBridgeResolutionTest.

5. **"Replay order in onHello" covered indirectly.** Driving onHello needs a ~25-member CompanionRepository fake plus a WatchLink interface refactor — out of scope and a large change to a shipped class. Covered as: `pending()` returns oldest-first (queue test), replay does not duplicate or refresh a queued entry (delivery test), and the flush-before-push ordering in onHello is source-level with the reason commented.

MINOR, unflagged in spec but changed because it went stale: `WatchPushResult.SentUnrecorded`'s doc said "Only the dismissal path can produce this" — now false, updated.

ONE JUDGEMENT CALL: `PendingQuestionResolutions.TTL_MS = CompanionConstants.QUESTION_STALE_AFTER_MS` rather than a second literal `21_600_000`. Same value, and the coupling is real — past the horizon the wrist card has already self-timed-out, so the entry has nothing left to resolve. Asserted at 21_600_000 in the test so a change to the shared constant still trips a gate.

<agent-message from="s3-eff">
EFFICIENCY REVIEW (wasted work only, no correctness findings). 7 findings, ranked.

**1. MEDIUM — every terminal:lifecycle event refetches terminal.list**
`apps/desktop/src/renderer/routes/_authenticated/_dashboard/v2-workspace/$workspaceId/hooks/useAutoAdoptBackgroundSessions/useAutoAdoptBackgroundSessions.ts:65-74`
Subscribed invalidate-only, no eventType filter, but only `created` is needed. `command-start` / `command-end` / synthesized prompt-redraw ends broadcast per foreground command on any OSC 133 shell (`packages/host-service/src/terminal/terminal.ts:3627,3640,3654`). Cost per event: `terminal.list` is not a cheap read — renderer→host RPC → `getDaemonClient()` + `daemon.list()` IPC round-trip + DB read (`terminal.ts:1060-1100`); then `isFetching` flips true→false = 2 re-renders of `V2WorkspaceCenter` (the query is tracked in this hook, mounted there); then the adopt effect re-runs (`sessions` is a new array) doing a localStorage parse, 2 Set builds and a tab walk. So a shell running 20 commands = 40 daemon round-trips + 80 center re-renders that can adopt nothing.
Also: grep shows no other lifecycle→`terminal.list.invalidate` in the tree, so the comment's "same shape `useTerminalAgentBindings` uses" isn't load-bearing cost precedent.
Cheaper: first line of the callback `if (payload.eventType !== "created") return;`. Keeps the created→pane invariant exactly; drops ~all volume. (`exit` already has its own consumers.)

**2. MEDIUM — sweep spawns a git process per project per boot, serially, unconditionally**
`packages/host-service/src/runtime/main-workspace-sweep.ts:31-62` (probe at :53)
`isGitRepoStrict` = `checkIsRepo()` = one git spawn. `ensureMainWorkspaceStrict` then spawns git AGAIN for the git path (`symbolic-ref --short HEAD`, ensure-main-workspace.ts:93-94). Boot git spawns for this sweep went 1×N → 2×N, serial, N = every project row ever. On Windows ARM64 a git spawn is ~50-150ms, so 30 projects ≈ +2-5s. Non-blocking for server start (background IIFE, `app.ts:219`) but it is `await`ed ahead of `runArchivedWorkspaceReconcile` (`app.ts:230` then `:238`), so the crash-recovery reconcile is delayed by the whole added time.
Cheaper, invariant-preserving:
 (a) bounded concurrency (pool of 4-8) over `rows` — loop bodies are independent per project; tri-state handling unchanged.
 (b) skip the probe entirely for projects that already have a `type='main'` row whose `branch !== NON_GIT_BRANCH` — one indexed SQL read replaces N spawns. Those rows take the git path today and, if the folder stopped being a repo, `ensureMainWorkspaceStrict` throws → wrapper swallows → row untouched: identical to pre-diff behaviour, no rewrite risk, so the fail-closed guarantee survives.
Do NOT reuse the TTL-cached `isGitRepo` — the diff's comment is right that it reintroduces the ambiguity.

**3. LOW-MEDIUM — modal fires host RPCs for probes master mode will never read**
`apps/desktop/.../DashboardNewWorkspaceModal/hooks/useMasterWorkspaceTarget/useMasterWorkspaceTarget.ts:28` → `useProjectGitState`
`resolveMasterMode` rungs 1-3 return `branch` for a cloud host, a remote host, or a multi-repo project — but `useProjectGitState`'s `enabled` doesn't know that. Selecting a REMOTE host with a main workspace issues a `git.isRepo` RPC across the relay to that host; `multi-repo-info` fires against the local host for any project with no local main (incl. while cloud is selected). `staleTime: Infinity` so it's once per key, not per render, but each is a network round-trip for a value rung 2 discards.
Cheaper: add an `enabled` param to `useProjectGitState` (default true, kanban unaffected) and pass `selectedHostId === machineId && selectedHostId !== CLOUD_HOST_ID` from `useMasterWorkspaceTarget`. Predicate order and fail-closed rungs untouched.

**4. LOW — three full scans of the workspace list per modal keystroke**
`useMasterWorkspaceTarget.ts:27, 28 (inside useProjectGitState:43), 36`
`useProjectMainWorkspaceId` is an unmemoized `workspaces.find` (`kanban/hooks/useProjectMainWorkspaceId/useProjectMainWorkspaceId.ts:22`) and is called twice — directly and again inside `useProjectGitState` — plus the `workspaces.find` at :36. `PromptGroup` re-renders per keystroke (`draft.prompt` is in the `useShallow` selector, `DashboardNewWorkspaceDraftContext.tsx:60`), so that's 3×O(W) over every workspace on every host, per character. Absolute cost small; it's pure duplication.
Cheaper: find the master ROW once in a `useMemo` here, derive `mainWorkspaceId` from it, and pass the id into `useProjectGitState` (or memoize inside `useProjectMainWorkspaceId`).

**5. LOW — duplicate live query over the same two collections in the same component**
`AgentHooks/hooks/useSurfaceHiddenMainWorkspaces/useSurfaceHiddenMainWorkspaces.ts:48-73` vs `AgentHooks/hooks/useSidebarMirrorSync/useSidebarMirrorSync.ts:459-491`
Both mounted in `AgentHooks`. The mirror's `v2WorkspaceLocalState` projection is a strict SUPERSET of the reconciler's (adds projectId/sectionId/tabOrder/pinnedAt), and its `v2SidebarProjects` projection is a superset too. Two incremental views maintained and two result arrays allocated for every curation change where one pair would do.
Cheaper: lift one pair of queries into `AgentHooks` (or a shared `useSidebarCurationRows`) and pass rows to both consumers. Note the mirror gates on its own `isReady`, so this is a small refactor, not a one-liner.

**6. LOW — reconciler materialises every workspace, then filters**
`useSurfaceHiddenMainWorkspaces.ts:35-44` and `:75-93`
The memo allocates W objects (all workspaces, all hosts) though only local `main`s can be candidates (~1 per project); the effect then builds a Map over ALL local-state rows and a Set over ALL sidebar projects before the first candidate check. Frequency is fine — `workspaces` is memoized upstream with react-query structural sharing, so the 30s poll (`useHostWorkspaces.ts:24,208`) doesn't churn identity, and the live queries emit on genuine leaf changes — so this runs per real change, not per render.
Cheaper: filter `type === "main" && hostId === machineId` inside the memo (the pure selector re-checks both, so the bucket-only predicate is unweakened), and early-return before building Map/Set when that list is empty — the normal case. Polish, not a hot path.
Also note: unlike the mirror, this hook has no debounce/signature gate; today's per-run cost doesn't justify one, but if the effect ever grows past Map/Set building the mirror's gate is the precedent.

**7. TRIVIAL — dead memo**
`useAutoAdoptBackgroundSessions.ts:66-68`: the `useCallback` is pointless — `useWorkspaceEvent` already wraps the callback in `useEffectEvent` (`renderer/hooks/host-service/useWorkspaceEvent/useWorkspaceEvent.ts:64`), so identity never matters. Inline it.

CHECKED, CLEAN: `created` broadcast volume (one per session create, at `terminal.ts:3759`, after the row commit — no fan-out concern); the eventBus re-narrow (`workspace-client/src/lib/eventBus.ts:290-325`) still allocates exactly one payload per event, the added arm is one comparison; `submitDisabledReason`'s new `draft.prompt`/`visibleFiles` deps recompute per keystroke but the body is a handful of comparisons and one `trim()`; `useProjectGitState`'s new `isError` derivation adds no query and no allocation; `restoreWorkspace` + `ensureWorkspaceInSidebar` on the master submit path are two collection writes on a user action.

DEVIATIONS (all additive, none behavioural against the spec)
1. NEW TEST SEAM __resetColdStartForTest() exported from resyncAgentStatus.ts. Required, not optional: coldStartDecided/sessionBeganCold latch once per module and the existing tests in that file seed the store before their first resync, which decides the session WARM for the whole file — so hostSessionBoundaries is never set and the relaunch report is unreachable without it. Sits beside the existing __resetRepairCooldownsForTest / __peekRepairOutcomeForTest seams.
2. Two extra exports on companionAlertSync.ts: hasReportedRelaunchBoundary(hostUrl) (the resync needs to read the latch) and resetRelaunchBoundaryLatchForTests().
3. retireReadyBefore ALSO validates its own boundary (integer, >0) and returns 0 + error log. The spec put validation in the index.ts sink; I kept that and added the manager-level guard so the state machine cannot be corrupted by a future caller. retireTerminal likewise refuses an empty id loudly.
4. VERDICT-MAP PRUNE TIMING, worth your eyes. "prune map entries for workspaces with no live alerts" is evaluated against the live set computed at the START of that same call, so a workspace that flipped and had all its rows retired keeps its `true` verdict until a LATER call sees nothing live for it, then drops it. Consequence: retiring does not immediately re-arm the same workspace for another flip. That is the conservative reading and it is pinned by two tests ("prunes the verdict of a workspace with nothing live left" and "keeps a verdict while the workspace still has a live alert"). Flag it if you meant the eager one.
5. lifecycleEvents.test.ts deliberately mocks ONLY renderer/lib/host-service-client. My first version also stubbed renderer/lib/trpc-client and renderer/lib/ringtones/play, and because Bun's mock.module is process-wide it took down HostNotificationSubscriber.test.tsx's keyboard-layout store on import when the directory ran as one job. Removed; a comment in the file says why.
6. The one existing test that pinned the OLD e-alert design ("does not touch an ERROR alert — reading a chat does not un-kill an agent") was rewritten to pin the new behaviour, with the 2026-08-20 user decision named in its doc comment.

Failure: phone holds a ready card for generation G2. A `c` for G1 (G1 < G2) arrives after the G2 row was evicted by CAPACITY or dropped as expired -> Unknown -> cancel t:X -> the unread G2 card comes down. Before, it did nothing. <agent-message from="s4-eff">
EFFICIENCY REVIEW (wasted work only; no correctness, no invariant weakening). 5 findings — 2 material, 3 cheap.

**1. MATERIAL — full sidebar-mirror read + curation index rebuilt once PER WORKSPACE, per mirror change.**
`C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\index.ts:1331` (probe closure) → `lifecycle-alerts.ts:285 isLifecycleWorkspaceCuratedOff` → `sidebar-filter.ts workspaceSidebarVerdict` → `createSidebarCuration`.
`retireCuratedOffAlerts` calls the closure once per distinct workspace holding a live alert. Each call does `db.readSidebarMirror()` = 3 SQLite statements + full `sidebar_workspaces` / `sidebar_projects` row arrays, then rebuilds 3 Maps/Sets over them (projects walked twice), then `db.findWorkspace(id)`. All W results are identical within one walk — same tick, same snapshot. Synchronous, on host-service's only thread, inside the `sidebarMirror.sync` mutation. W is bounded by distinct workspaces among ≤512 live alerts. The file's own comment at `lifecycle-alerts.ts:303` already calls this read expensive (it's why the sweep probe caches).
Cheaper: read the snapshot + build the curation ONCE at the top of the sink (after the mirror write committed — same freshness, cache still bypassed), then the closure is `curation.workspaceVerdict(db.findWorkspace(id))`. W full mirror reads → 1. Also hoists the per-call `Date.now()`.

**2. MATERIAL — `SharedPreferences.commit()` on the UI thread, twice per question resolution.**
`C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\superset-companion\app-phone\src\main\java\family\khaira\supersetcompanion\phone\wear\WatchBridge.kt:1040` (`queue.enqueue`) and `:1049` (`queue.clear`) run on the CALLER's dispatcher — `deliverResolutionThrough` never hops. `WatchLink.sendResolved` hops to `Dispatchers.IO` (WatchLink.kt:119) so the send is fine; the two blocking disk writes are not. Two of the three senders are `viewModelScope.launch` = `Dispatchers.Main.immediate`: `ConversationViewModel.kt:518` (every phone answer) and `SessionViewModel.kt:231` (`sweepNotifications`, which loops `stale.forEach` → 2×N serial main-thread commits).
Each `write` also re-encodes and re-persists the WHOLE list. The dismissal queue uses the identical commit() pattern safely because its only entry points are FCM / Data-Layer threads (`WatchBridge.kt:362`, `:380`); the resolution queue is the first one with viewmodel callers.
Cheaper, durability untouched: `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)` around the enqueue and the clear inside `deliverResolutionThrough`. Keep `commit()`.

**3. `flushQuestionResolutions` re-enqueues entries it just read off disk.**
`WatchBridge.kt:416-428` → `deliverResolutionThrough` → `queue.enqueue` per entry. Every one of those ids is already on disk (they came from `pending(nowMs)` at :417), so the enqueue is a full read+parse to discover "already present" — pure waste, N times. Then each delivery does its own `clear` = another full read + full-list commit. N entries → up to 2N commits and O(N²) encode/decode work; N bounded by CAPACITY 256, on the Data Layer callback thread.
Cheaper: skip the enqueue on the replay path (flag or a separate send step), and batch-clear the delivered ids in one write after the loop.

**4. `retireCuratedOffAlerts` walks the whole alert map again per flipped workspace.**
`lifecycle-alerts.ts:1510` — `[...alerts.values()]` inside the per-workspace loop: a fresh ≤512-element array copy plus a full scan for each flipped workspace, after the pass at `:1487` already visited every alert. O(W×A) where one pass would do.
Cheaper: in the `:1487` pass build `Map<hostWorkspaceId, HeldAlert[]>` of the live rows alongside `liveWorkspaces`, then the retire loop iterates only that workspace's bucket. Same rows, same order, no copy.

**5. Trivial — `document.hasFocus()` evaluated before two free predicates.**
`...\V2NotificationController\lib\lifecycleEvents.ts:344`. The hop's condition runs `isUserPresent()` (`:466`, reads `document.hidden` + `document.hasFocus()`) before `payload.eventType === "Stop" || "Failed"` (`:345`) and `transition.axes === null`. Most lifecycle events are neither Stop nor Failed, so the DOM call is paid to be discarded. Note `shouldSuppress` (`:476`) already calls `isUserPresent()` on the same live-event path → 2 `hasFocus()` per event today. Cost is sub-microsecond either way; reordering the `&&` chain (eventType and axes first) is free. No action needed beyond that.

**Checked and clean (no waste):**
- Exit-event sink: `event-bus.ts` → `recordCompanionTerminalGone` → `supersede` = one array copy + one scan of ≤512 per confirmed pty exit, at user-action frequency. Fine. (All three retire paths full-scan because `alerts` isn't indexed by terminal — pre-existing, and not worth an index at this cap.)
- `retireReadyBefore` (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:1436`): one copy + one scan, once per host per cold launch. Fine.
- Mirror sink frequency is already gated on `changed` (`sidebar-mirror.ts:294`), so the 5-minute heartbeat re-push costs nothing — finding 1 only fires on real curation writes.
- `questionStaleAtMs` / `questionTimeoutAfterMs`: two arithmetic ops at post time. Fine.
- `retractionCancelKey` / `RetractionVerdict`: allocation-free (`data object` for Stale/Unknown, one small `Retired` per retraction). Fine.
- Renderer relaunch-boundary report: Set lookup + at most one mutation per host per launch. Fine.

**F1 — FEATURES.md:23-24: one feature, two manifest rows, at two altitudes.**

**F3 — `resyncAgentStatus.ts:81,445,806`: `ResyncResult.relaunchBoundaryReported` has no production reader.**
All five reads are in `resyncAgentStatus.test.ts:683-758`. The other `ResyncResult` fields are counters a caller logs; this one is a test assertion in the shape of an API.

**F4 — Boundary validation is four layers, and only two own a distinct aspect.**
integer-and-positive is asserted at `companionAlertSync.ts:514` (`Number.isSafeInteger && > 0`), `companion.ts:293` (`z.number().int().positive().finite()`), `index.ts:1002` (`!Number.isInteger || <= 0 || > nowMs`), and `lifecycle-alerts.ts` `retireReadyBefore` (`!Number.isInteger || <= 0`).
Two are real: the renderer pre-check keeps a fractional value off a promise contracted never to reject (and matches its sibling `reportTerminalSeen`), and the sink's `> nowMs` is the semantic check nothing else can make. The other two are shape checks behind a Zod schema that already made them: the sink's `Number.isInteger`/`<= 0` half, and the manager's guard entirely — `retireReadyBefore`'s only caller is that sink.
Same pattern in `retireTerminal`: `typeof hostTerminalId !== "string" || length === 0` guards against a value TypeScript already types as `string`, arriving from a discriminated-union field on the event bus.
Right form: tRPC owns shape, the sink owns range, the manager trusts its caller. The manager is not an API boundary — it is one process-local module called by one other.

**F5 — `index.ts:1309-1355`: the curated-off retirement puts freshness policy, the db call shape, and the fail-closed rule in the bridge-wiring file.**
`lifecycle-alerts.ts` `retireCuratedOffAlerts(isCuratedOffFresh)` takes a probe per call because the manager's held `deps.isCuratedOff` caches a `true` for 30 s. index.ts satisfies that by calling `isLifecycleWorkspaceCuratedOff({db, organizationId, hostWorkspaceId, nowMs})` inline, inside its own try/catch, with its own fail-closed decision and log line.
Misplaced: the cached probe's mirror-image rule (fail *open*, log, don't cache) lives in `createLifecycleCurationProbe` in `lifecycle-alerts.ts`. Its twin now lives 900 lines away in a factory function. The bridge is also the only caller that has to know `isLifecycleWorkspaceCuratedOff`'s db-shaped signature — it is exported from lifecycle-alerts for lifecycle-alerts' own use.
Right form: both probes come from `lifecycle-alerts.ts` (a `cache: false` option on `createLifecycleCurationProbe`, or a named sibling factory), both go in `deps`, and `retireCuratedOffAlerts()` takes no argument. The sink then says "the mirror changed" and nothing about how curation is read or what a throw means.
Note the contrast the brief asked about: the event-bus hook (`event-bus.ts:246-248`) is exactly right — a two-condition gate and one call, no logic. The mirror-change hook is the one that leaked.

**F6 — `lifecycleEvents.ts:434-446`: `updateV2AgentLifecycleStatus` is a pass-through that owns nothing and now forwards five arguments.**
Its entire body is `updatePaneStatus(workspaceId, payload, target, paneLayout, fromReplay)`. It converts a named-object call into a positional one and adds nothing. The new flag makes it a fifth positional and a fifth line of boilerplate in the object type above it.
Right form: delete it; give `updatePaneStatus` the object signature and let its two callers call it directly. Pre-existing, but the diff is what makes it visible.

**F7 — `lifecycleEvents.ts:73`: the comment claims a uniqueness the code doesn't have.**
"The live bus. This is the one path the visible-clear hop may fire on." — `V2NotificationController.tsx:198` also passes `fromReplay: false` (the Electron fallback), so there are two. The parameter's own docblock is accurate; this one is the comment a future caller reads while deciding what to pass.
Right form: "A live event, not a replay" — same as the fallback's comment.

**F8 — Companion, `ShownLifecycleAlerts.kt` + `CompanionNotifications.kt:314` + `LifecycleNotifier.kt:202`: `retractionCancelKey(verdict, terminalId)` makes both surfaces thread `terminalId` twice.**
`forgetRetracted` already receives `terminalId`; the verdict it hands back must then be re-paired with the same `terminalId` at the cancel site, and both surfaces have to remember to pass the same one.
The seam itself is right — one decision in `:core`, returning a key each surface addresses in its own id space, is the correct answer to "the phone and the watch must not diverge". It is one notch too low: the record was asked about a terminal and knows the answer, but hands back a verdict that can't be acted on alone.
Right form: `RetractionVerdict.Unknown(val terminalKey: String?)` carrying what the record was asked about, or `forgetRetracted` returning the cancel key already resolved. Cancel site becomes `verdict.cancelKey?.let { manager.cancel(it, ...) }` with no second argument to get wrong.

**F9 — Companion, `WatchBridge.kt`: the two "opposite number" paths are shaped differently.**
Dismissals: `deliverDismissal` (line 389), a private method that does the work. Resolutions: a private `deliverResolution` (line 445) that immediately delegates to top-level `internal suspend deliverResolutionThrough(message, nowMs, queue, logRefusal = {Log.e(...)}, send)` (line 1030) — three of whose five parameters exist so a JVM test can drive it, including a default-valued logger.
The docblock calls it "[deliverDismissal]'s opposite number", but a reader comparing them finds two shapes and has to work out that the difference is testing, not design. `logRefusal` in particular is a parameter with exactly one non-default caller, and that caller is a test.
Right form: pick one shape for the pair. Either lift the dismissal path to the same top-level seam, or test the resolution path the way the dismissal path is tested (through `PendingQuestionResolutions` directly, which already has its own JVM test) and let `deliverResolution` be a private method like its twin.

<agent-message from="s4-simple">
SIMPLIFICATION REVIEW (quality only, no correctness, no behavior changes). 10 findings, 1 major.

=== 1. MAJOR — the curation verdict map is inert; the flip machinery is unobservable ===
`packages/host-service/src/companion/lifecycle-alerts.ts:624` (decl), `:1485-1565` (fn), eager delete at `:1541`.

You asked whether eager delete + lazy prune are both needed. They are — but only to sustain each other. The bigger result: `previous` is provably always `false`, so the whole map is dead state.

Proof. Map starts empty. Only writer is `:1507` `set(ws, current)`. Reader is `:1503` `get(ws) ?? false`.
- `current === false` → stores `false`.
- `current === true` + `previous === false` → flip branch → retires → `delete` at `:1541`. Entry gone.
- `current === true` + `previous === true` → `continue` at `:1508`, `true` survives. But `previous === true` requires an earlier surviving `true`, and by induction none exists.

So the map only ever holds `false`, `previous` is a constant, and `if (!current || previous) continue` reduces to `if (!current) continue`. The function is already a pure state test: "any live alert in a workspace the fresh probe calls curated-off gets retired."

The three tests agree. `:1916` "retires only on a false-to-true FLIP, not on every mirror write" passes under a state test too — the third walk returns 0 because every alert is now `retracted` and `liveWorkspaces` is empty, not because a verdict was remembered. Same for `:1933`. `:1997` and `:2029` pin the eager delete, i.e. the thing that kills the flip.

Cost: ~25 lines of code + ~55 lines of comment (`:608-623`, `:1462-1483`, `:1528-1540`, `:1553-1560`) defending a distinction the code cannot make, plus 4 tests documenting it.

Simpler form — pick one, not both:
```ts
retireCuratedOffAlerts(isCuratedOffFresh) {
  if (stopped) return 0;
  const live = [...alerts.values()].filter(isLiveAlert);
  let retired = 0;
  for (const ws of new Set(live.map((a) => a.hostWorkspaceId))) {
    if (!isCuratedOffFresh(ws)) continue;
    for (const a of live.filter((a) => a.hostWorkspaceId === ws)) {
      retireAlert(a, { kind: "curated-off" });
      retired += 1;
    }
    deps.logger.info(...);
  }
  return retired;
}
```
Map, eager delete, lazy prune, and the flip commentary all go. Or, if the flip really is wanted, drop `:1541` and rewrite the tests to actually distinguish it. Currently you pay for the flip design and get the state design.

Sub-finding: the live-state predicate `state === "sending" || "sent" || "standing"` is written out twice (`:1487-1494`, `:1516-1523`) and again as a negation. One `isLiveAlert(alert)` helper.

=== 2. `fromReplay` threads through a pre-existing no-op wrapper ===
`apps/desktop/.../lib/lifecycleEvents.ts:432-446`.

`updateV2AgentLifecycleStatus` is a pure pass-through: destructures 5 fields, calls `updatePaneStatus` with the same 5 reordered. Pre-existing (`489f314942:354-366`), but it is the third layer you asked about.

Narrowest seam: the flag itself is right — 3 call sites, 2 `false` / 1 `true`, single consumer at `:342`. Cost is the free extra hop. Inline the wrapper → `fromReplay` threads 2 layers instead of 3, and `updatePaneStatus`'s 5-positional-arg signature (odd in a file that is otherwise object-params) can become an object at the same time.

=== 3. Relaunch-boundary trio: 3 exports where 1 + a test seam would do ===
`apps/desktop/.../lib/companionAlertSync.ts:507`, `:533`, `:538`; caller `resyncAgentStatus.ts:803-810`.

The latch Set lives in `companionAlertSync`, and `reportRelaunchBoundary` already writes it — but the *read* is exported so the caller can gate. Check-and-add split across a module boundary.

The only reason the caller must know is `result.relaunchBoundaryReported` (`resyncAgentStatus.ts:73-79`, `:445`, `:806`) — a field on a production result type with **no production reader**; grep finds 5 reads, all in `resyncAgentStatus.test.ts`. Tests could assert on the `retireStaleReadyAlerts` mock call count instead, which is what they actually mean.

Minor: `resetRelaunchBoundaryLatchForTests` (`companionAlertSync.ts:538`) and `__resetColdStartForTest` (`resyncAgentStatus.ts:333`) are the same kind of seam, added in the same change, under two different naming conventions. Pick one.

=== 4. `retireReadyBefore` — four validations, three of them the same rule ===
1. `companionAlertSync.ts:513` — `Number.isSafeInteger(boundaryMs) || <= 0`
2. `trpc/router/companion/companion.ts:292` — `z.number().int().positive().finite()`
3. `companion/index.ts:1003-1008` — `!Number.isInteger || <= 0 || > nowMs`
4. `lifecycle-alerts.ts:1437-1445` — `!Number.isInteger || <= 0`

Only #3's `> nowMs` (future boundary) carries information the others don't. #4 is a strict subset of #3, and #3 is its only caller — same rule twice, not defense in depth. #2 is the genuine wire boundary and should stay.

Simpler form: give the manager the whole rule (it needs a clock — pass `deps.now`) and make the sink a thin call; or drop #4 and let the sink own it. Either way one place answers "is this boundary usable". Also `.int()` already implies finite — `.finite()` at `:293` is a no-op modifier.

Minor: both `retireReadyBefore` (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:1461`) and its sink (`index.ts:1016`) log the same `boundaryMs` + `retired`; same for `retireCuratedOffAlerts` (`:1546` per-workspace, `index.ts:1352` total). Two lines per event, same numbers.

=== 5. Kotlin: `ResolutionDelivery` / `asPushResult` / `unrecordedResolutionReason` — 3 names for what the dismissal path does with 0 ===
`app-phone/.../wear/WatchBridge.kt:1003`, `:1030-1059`, `:1068-1070`, `:1078-1083`; plus the 1-line private hop at `:447-451`.

Compare `pushLifecycleDismiss` (`:355-368`): enqueue, send, build the reason string once, log it, return `Sent` / `SentUnrecorded`. One function, no intermediate type. The resolution path does the identical job across four named pieces.

`ResolutionDelivery(send, recorded)` is isomorphic to `WatchPushResult.Sent | SentUnrecorded` — `recorded` is exactly the tag. `asPushResult` is the isomorphism. `unrecordedResolutionReason` exists only so the log line and the result reason match, which is free if built once at one site.

Simpler form: `deliverResolutionThrough(...): WatchPushResult`. Build the reason inline, log it, return `SentUnrecorded(result, reason)` or `Sent(result)`. `WatchPushResult` is a plain sealed interface with no Android deps, so the JVM test seam is unaffected — the 12 tests in `WatchBridgeResolutionTest.kt` assert on `Sent`/`SentUnrecorded` instead of `.send`/`.recorded`. Drops 3 names, ~25 lines, and makes the two hop functions read alike.

=== 6. Kotlin: `PendingQuestionResolutions` is a near-verbatim copy of `PendingLifecycleDismissals` ===

=== 7. Kotlin: `questionStaleAtMs` round-trips now→absolute→duration on 2 of 3 call sites ===

`questionStaleAtMs` disappears; one helper instead of two, and the "conversion at the last possible moment" argument in the `:96-99` KDoc holds unchanged.

<agent-message from="impl-envfix">
(WIN-USER-ENV) done. Not committed. 7 files modified, 3 new.

ROOT CAUSE (exact)
`packages/host-service/src/terminal/clean-shell-env.ts:spawnCleanShellEnv()` runs
`spawn(resolveConfiguredShell(), ["-i","-l","-c", 'echo -n "__SUPERSET_SHELL_ENV__"; command env; ...'])`.
On win32 `resolveConfiguredShell` returns `%COMSPEC%` = `C:\Windows\system32\cmd.exe`. cmd.exe takes no `-` switches and no POSIX body: it ignores the args, prints its banner, finds stdin at EOF (child stdio is "ignore"), exits **0**. Exit 0 routes to the success path, `parseEnvOutput` finds no delimiter in `Microsoft Windows [Version …]` and throws. So it was not a flaky parse — the probe never ran a command at all, on every Windows boot since the file existed. The catch in `terminal/env.ts:93` then pinned the whole process to `process.env`, i.e. to the parent's env block. Fixing the parse buys nothing: cmd.exe has no rc files, so there is no login-shell env to recover.

WHAT CHANGED
1. NEW `packages/shared/src/windows-user-env.ts` — reads `HKCU\Environment` via async `execFile` on an absolute `%SystemRoot%\System32\reg.exe` (no PATH dependency, no native deps), parses the 4-space `name/TYPE/value` format, merges UNDER a target env. process.env always wins; case-folded like Windows (registry `Path` cannot shadow `PATH`); `REG_EXPAND_SZ` `%VAR%` expanded single-pass, unresolvable refs left literal; non-string reg types and empty values skipped. Missing header → throws (unrecognized output ≠ empty env); header with no values → `[]`. Read failure is `console.error`-loud and returns `{ok:false}` rather than bricking boot. **Never logs values** — that key holds the user's API keys; names and counts only. Injected `run` for tests. Exported as `@superset/shared/windows-user-env` (exports-map entry only, no dependency change — override/lock gate still green).
2. `clean-shell-env.ts` — `getStrictShellEnvironment()` now branches on win32 to `resolveWindowsShellEnv()`: process.env snapshot + registry fill, no cmd.exe spawn. Kills the every-boot error and gives PTYs the registry vars too.
3. Merge reaches the gate at three boot points, all awaited:
   - `apps/desktop/src/main/host-service/index.ts` main() first statement — **this is the entry production runs**, so this is the one that fixes the incident.
   - `packages/host-service/src/serve.ts` main() first statement (standalone/CLI).
   - `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` right after `await app.whenReady()`, before the coordinator spawns the child (`...process.env`) and before keep-awake's own `SUPERSET_COMPANION_BRIDGE` short-circuit in `lib/keep-awake/companion-gate.ts` — otherwise the power hold stays released and the phone still can't reach a sleeping laptop.
   No spot-patch on `isCompanionBridgeEnabled()`; it reads env at gate time and now sees a corrected env. Relay/proxy/CA/token flags benefit the same way.
   Documented gap: vars consumed by the import-time env schema (`host-service/src/env.ts`) are read before main() and are NOT covered — all coordinator-supplied (PORT, ORGANIZATION_ID, HOST_DB_PATH), never registry-sourced.
4. FEATURES.md — new row "Windows user-environment merge" + 5 per-file `(WIN-USER-ENV)` marker lines (per-file pinning, mirroring `(COMPANION-BRIDGE-MOUNT)`, so a merge dropping any one merge point fails the gate).

TESTS — 32 new/covering, 0 fail
- `packages/shared/src/windows-user-env.test.ts` — 17 pass (parse of real CRLF reg output, spaces-in-values, expand rules, merge-under, case-insensitivity, injected-reader failure).
- `packages/host-service/src/companion/windows-user-env-gate.test.ts` — 4 pass. The required one: parent env lacks the var + registry has it → `isCompanionBridgeEnabled()` false → true. Plus registry cannot re-enable a launcher-disabled `=0`, and no-op off win32.
- `clean-shell-env.test.ts` — +2 win32 tests (11 pass total): snapshot resolves without touching cmd.exe, process.env stays authoritative.

LIVE PROOF on this machine (real registry, then cleaned up): deleted `SUPERSET_COMPANION_BRIDGE` from a live process env → gate `false` → `applyWindowsUserEnvToProcess()` filled exactly 1 var → gate `true`.

VERIFICATION

<agent-message from="rev-envfix-opus">
(WIN-USER-ENV) adversarial review of the uncommitted working-tree diff. Verified: 32 tests green, (REFERR-GATE) clean, marker gate 260/260, override-consistency OK, no type errors in the new module. Two real parse defects proven empirically on this machine.

[MAJOR] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\shared\src\windows-user-env.ts:194-206
Defect: reg.exe transcodes values to the console output code page before writing to the pipe, so `encoding: "utf8"` decodes bytes that are already lossy. Non-ASCII registry values are silently corrupted.
Failure: Proven on this box. `chcp` = 437. `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\DesktopSpotlight\DefaultCreatives` holds U+00A9 at index 456 (confirmed via the registry API); `reg.exe query` on the same key emits ZERO bytes >= 0x80 and renders it as ASCII `c`. So on a machine whose profile is `C:\Users\José\...` (or any CJK username), registry `JAVA_HOME` / `ANDROID_HOME` / `OneDrive` come back as `Jose...` / `Jos?...` / `?????`. Those are exactly the vars the merge applies (absent from the installer-relaunch env block — the feature's whole reason to exist), so the app and every terminal get paths that do not resolve. No error, no log line: the exact silent degradation the module's header says it removes.
Fix: stop parsing reg.exe console output. Read the user hive through a UTF-8 channel — `powershell -NoProfile -NonInteractive -Command "[Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariables('User') | ConvertTo-Json -Compress"`, or force the code page for the child (`cmd /d /c chcp 65001 >nul & reg query ...`). If neither is acceptable, detect U+FFFD / a non-65001 console CP and fail loud rather than applying mangled values.

[MAJOR] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\shared\src\windows-user-env.ts:70,92-124
Defect: The line regex assumes one value per line. A REG_SZ containing a newline is truncated at the first newline, and its continuation lines are parsed as additional variables.
Failure: Proven against the shipped parser. Input rendering a two-line `MY_CERT` value yields `{name:"MY_CERT", value:"-----BEGIN CERT-----"}` — remainder dropped, no throw. Input where a value's second physical line reads `    NODE_OPTIONS    REG_SZ    --require C:\evil.js` yields a real `NODE_OPTIONS` entry, and `mergeWindowsUserEnv` writes it into the target: `{"PATH":"C:\\Windows","NOTES":"harmless first line","NODE_OPTIONS":"--require C:\\evil.js"}`. reg.exe prints embedded newlines raw — verified on `HKCU\...\DesktopSpotlight\State`, whose REG_SZ spans 13 output lines — and its output is byte-identical for "one two-line value" and "two values", so the parser cannot tell them apart. Half of this is a silent wrong value; the other half lets a single multi-line value (a pasted PEM, a JSON blob) define arbitrary extra variables in the Electron main process env, which host-service-coordinator.ts:835 then spreads into the host-service child via `...process.env`.
Fix: do not shell out to reg.exe's text output for this. If it stays, treat any line that does not match the value-line shape as a continuation of the previous entry's value, and reject the whole read when the reconstruction is ambiguous.

[MINOR] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\shared\src\windows-user-env.ts:187-192
Defect: `resolveRegExePath()` falls back to a bare `"reg.exe"` — PATH-resolved — when SystemRoot is unset, giving up the absoluteness the adjacent comment says is the point.
Failure: The fallback fires precisely in the degraded-parent-env case the module exists for. A relaunch handing down a PATH whose first entry is user-writable executes that `reg.exe` instead. Same-user, so no privilege boundary is crossed, but it is a silent default where the module's own policy is fail-loud.
Fix: throw when SystemRoot is missing, or hardcode `C:\Windows\System32\reg.exe` as the fallback.

[MINOR] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\shared\src\windows-user-env.ts:267-272
Defect: The NEVER-LOG-VALUES invariant is enforced only by the header comment; no test pins it. The log line does enumerate variable NAMES.
Failure: `HKCU\Environment` on this machine holds RENDER_API_KEY, NEON_API_KEY, NEW_RELIC_API_KEY, VOYAGEAI_API_KEY, MILVUS_TOKEN, GH_TOKEN, CF_API_TOKEN, WEBSHARE_KEY(S) and XERO_CLIENT_SECRET. After an installer relaunch the merge writes that whole inventory of names into the desktop/host-service log. No secret leaves, but a future edit switching `applied` to entries would, and nothing would catch it. This repo enforces comparable invariants with tripwire tests (the companion-alert key-set and byte-budget tests).
Fix: add a test that captures the log call and asserts no entry value appears in it.

[MINOR] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\shared\src\windows-user-env.test.ts:173-184
Defect: Test gaps on the paths that actually broke.
Failure: No case for a multi-line value, none for non-ASCII, and the default `defaultRegQueryRunner` is never exercised. "Reports a read failure loudly" asserts only `result.ok === false`, never that `console.error` fired — the loud half of the documented contract is untested and would pass if the error log were deleted.
Fix: add fixtures for the two parse cases above and spy on console.error in the failure test.

[MINOR] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\terminal\clean-shell-env.ts:250-267
Defect: The registry is read twice per host-service boot with no shared cache.
Failure: `main()` at apps\desktop\src\main\host-service\index.ts:51 reads it, then `resolveTerminalBaseEnv()` reaches `resolveWindowsShellEnv()` which reads it again. The second read is a guaranteed no-op (the first already filled process.env, which this copies), so it buys nothing and costs a second reg.exe spawn — up to a second `REG_QUERY_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s) if reg.exe is wedged or AV-delayed. The desktop-main call at apps\desktop\src\main\index.ts:405 sits on the critical path to `installEgressFence()` and the first window, so the same 5 s worst case defers window creation.
Fix: memoize the parsed entries in the module and have `resolveWindowsShellEnv` reuse them.

Checked and clean: the boot-ordering claims (the companion gate is read at call time — companion/index.ts:1767, keep-awake/companion-gate.ts:210, keep-awake/presence-beacon.ts:266 — so the fix genuinely reaches it, and the coordinator does spread `...process.env` at host-service-coordinator.ts:835); the win32 premise (`resolveConfiguredShell` returns `%COMSPEC%`, so the cmd.exe diagnosis is correct); merge-direction and case-folding against real `reg query` output shapes (four-space separators, spaces in values, REG_DWORD and empty-value skipping all match this machine's actual output); no new PTY leak (`stripTerminalRuntimeEnv` is a denylist applied after the merge and already covers NODE_OPTIONS, BROWSER_BRIDGE_SECRET, HOST_*); no env reaches Sentry or the companion wire; egress-fence ordering is unaffected (the fence only observes the renderer session, which does not exist during the await); `@superset/shared` is a declared dependency of both consumers and the new exports-map entry resolves. The new files are CRLF in the working tree but `core.autocrlf=true` normalizes them on commit, so biome's format complaint is local noise, not a defect.

Verdict: FIX-FIRST
</agent-message>

<agent-message from="rev-lostcap-fable">
Adversarial review of the uncommitted lost-contact 6h cap diff in C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\superset-companion. One MINOR finding, verdict SHIP.

[MINOR] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\superset-companion\app-phone\src\main\java\family\khaira\supersetcompanion\phone\notifications\CompanionNotifications.kt:401
Defect: setOnlyAlertOnce(true) suppresses the intended 6-hour re-alert whenever the notification is still on screen, and claimPostStyle() has already spent the window on that soundless post.
Failure: continuous outage, user never swipes the warning. Silent re-posts every 15 min keep it showing. At T+6h claimPostStyle() returns ALERT and records a new window, but the post is an update to a still-showing notification, so FLAG_ONLY_ALERT_ONCE mutes it — no buzz, window consumed anyway. If the desktop then briefly reconnects (CANCEL takes the notification down) and dies again at T+6h30m, that genuinely re-raised outage is SILENT until T+12h. Contradicts the code's own claim-ordering principle at line 377 ("a claim spent on a post that then does not go out would silence the next real one") and the pinned test name "a warning after six hours alerts again", which does not hold audibly on device in this state. Within the user's "once every six hours max" spec (failure direction is under-alerting), hence MINOR not MAJOR.
Fix: tie the flag to the style — .setOnlyAlertOnce(style == LostContactPostStyle.SILENT) — or claim the window only when the post will actually alert as a fresh notification.

Checked clean:
- Claim ordering / double-buzz race: single process confirmed (no android:process in manifest; CompanionContainer.get is a @Volatile double-checked singleton; WorkManager runs in the default process). Both trigger paths (SessionViewModel.kt:280 foreground collector, BackgroundLiveness.kt:143 worker) reach the ONE LostContactAlerts instance. The unsynchronized read-then-write in claimPostStyle can in theory double-ALERT across threads, but both posts target the same tag/id, so the second lands as an update and onlyAlertOnce mutes it — no double buzz is reachable. Cross-process SharedPreferences staleness is moot.
- setSilent on minSdk 33: androidx API-26+ branch clears sound/vibration and sets GROUP_ALERT_SUMMARY with implicit "silent" group; only setSilent usage in the app, no grouping interference; works against the IMPORTANCE_DEFAULT liveness channel.
- Boundary math: >= alerts at exactly 6h — one buzz per 6h satisfies "max". Backwards clock alerts and re-anchors to the earlier now; worst case one extra buzz, correct failure direction.
- Permission path: canPost() returns before the claim, so a dropped post never spends the window.

Empirical results already banked (registry left clean, verified — every test value I wrote to HKCU\Environment was removed and confirmed gone):
- The PowerShell reader kills all three majors: `José™©—日本` round-trips byte-exact UTF-8 (hex identical, checked with xxd on raw pipe bytes); a multiline value stays ONE value and an embedded `    NODE_OPTIONS    REG_SZ    --require C:\evil.js` line is NOT injected as a key; a leading-space name stays `" SUPERSET_..."` and does not collapse onto the real variable; `SAFE    REG_SZ    DECOY` stays a value.
- `[Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariables('User')` is the WRONG API: it stringifies everything, so a REG_MULTI_SZ arrives as the literal `"System.String[]"` and a REG_DWORD as `"7"`. Reading the key directly and filtering on `GetValueKind` skips both cleanly.
- It also pre-expands REG_EXPAND_SZ, which would silently erase the REG_SZ/REG_EXPAND_SZ distinction and wrongly expand a literal `pa%TEMP%ss` REG_SZ value. So the script I settled on emits `{"s":{...},"e":{...}}` — REG_SZ verbatim, REG_EXPAND_SZ RAW via `DoNotExpandEnvironmentNames` — which lets me do minor #5's two-pass expansion in TS against the merged target, so a same-hive `%ZBASE%` resolves regardless of order while a REG_SZ literal `%` is never touched. Verified: empty hive emits `{"s":{},"e":{}}`, single entry stays an object, no BOM.
</agent-message>
