<agent-message from="impl-lostcap">
Both round-3 MAJORs fixed. `:app-phone:testDebugUnitTest` — **316 tests, 0 failures** on a forced rerun (was 312; +4, 24 now in `LostContactRateLimitTest`). Not committed.

**1. Foreground 6h re-alert never fired.** Confirmed and fixed. Went with the tick, not a boundary-scheduled wake-up.

Added a `repostTick` field to `LostContactObservation` that is folded in **only while `lostContact` is true**, and a new `internal fun lostContactObservations(observations, ticks)` that does the combine + `distinctUntilChanged`. During an outage each tick makes the observation genuinely different so the limiter is asked again; while healthy every observation collapses to `LOST_CONTACT_NO_TICK` and the stream is exactly as quiet as before, so no cancel-every-15-minutes churn against a good desktop.

`LOST_CONTACT_REPOST_TICK_MS = 900_000L` — 15 minutes, matching `BackgroundLivenessWorker`'s period, so foreground and background re-evaluate the cap at the same cadence and the boundary re-alert is at most 15 min late.

`LostContactObservation` moved out of `SessionViewModel` (was a `private` nested class) into `LostContactPolicy.kt`; the shaper is top-level `internal` for the same reason `deliverResolutionThrough` is — a JVM test can drive 13 hours through it in milliseconds with no ViewModel.

Consequence worth naming: during an outage the warning is now silently re-posted every 15 min in the foreground, which also puts it back if the user swiped it away. That is exactly what the background path has always done; this only makes foreground match. Documented at the shaper.

Three flow tests: 13h continuous outage with unchanged counts → **52 emissions, 3 ALERTs** (0h/6h/12h); 10 ticks while healthy → 1 emission; a counts change still gets through on its own. **Verified non-vacuous** — I reverted the fold to always-`NO_TICK` and re-ran: `a continuous foreground outage alerts at each six-hour boundary FAILED`, then restored.

**2. Failed-write recovery was false for the real store.** You were right about AOSP — `commit()` runs `commitToMemory()` before `enqueueDiskWrite` and never rolls back.

The real store now removes the key after a failed commit: `prefs.edit().remove(KEY).apply()`. `apply` rather than `commit` because only the memory half is load-bearing (the disk is already refusing writes, blocking on a second failure buys nothing). Wiping the key from disk if that write does land is harmless — the value it replaces was already old enough to alert on, which is precisely why this claim got as far as trying to write.

Traced the persistent-failure case to be sure it does not reopen the spam: claim decides ALERT, write fails, rollback, `recordAlert` returns false, `claimPostStyle` returns SILENT. Every subsequent claim repeats that. Always silent, never spam — bounded and visible.

Also promoted this to an explicit contract on `LostContactAlertStore.recordAlert`: *after a false, `lastAlertAtMs()` must report exactly what it reported before the call*, with a note that a memory-first store has to roll back itself because SharedPreferences does not.

The fake now models the real semantics — `lastAlertAtMs = atMs` first, then roll back and return false if unwritable — so it fails for the same reason production would. **Verified non-vacuous**: removing the rollback from the fake fails three tests (`an alert that cannot be written down is posted silently instead`, `a claim alerts again once the disk recovers`, and the new `a failed write leaves the reported window exactly as it was`), then restored.

Honest limit: SharedPreferences is an android.jar stub in JVM tests, so the real store's rollback is asserted at the contract level, not against the framework. Robolectric or an androidTest would be the only way to close that, and neither exists here.

**Two tidy-ups caught on final read:** my clock property had landed between `watchLostContact`'s KDoc and the function (KDoc was documenting the wrong declaration) — moved above it; and two imports were out of lexicographic order — fixed. No lines over 100 chars added.

**One thing I did NOT touch, flagging rather than fixing:** `watchLostContact`'s KDoc still ends "A background worker is a separate piece of work." That worker now exists (`BackgroundLivenessWorker`). Pre-existing staleness, outside this change — say the word and I will correct it.
